14:T424,## Problem

A real FormService.startSession call with AgentRuntime and plugin-sql PGlite fails before persistence. The form serializer deliberately returns a null-prototype record; Drizzle inspects the top-level component data value and assumes a prototype. In-memory component fixtures did not exercise this boundary.

## Scope and acceptance

Preserve the safe public serializer and its own-key handling. Publish an adapter-compatible ordinary top-level JSON envelope for session, submission and autofill component writes, without invoking untrusted getters or changing nested values. Do not alter the general SQL adapter or public form lifecycle.

Verify actual FormService start/update/stash/restore/submission/autofill against real PGlite; inspect saved rows and adversarial own-key roundtrips. Include full form package tests/typecheck/lint and root verification. No schema migration or external integration is required.

Audited base b676280384b15df54204276e34bb8740db26936f. Found while independently validating #30757 with the real database boundary.
15:T773,**Description**

`PlanningService._execute_parallel` constructs an `_execute_step` coroutine for every item in `plan.steps` and submits the complete list to `asyncio.gather`. The planner's default prompt suggests 10 steps, but `max_steps` is configurable and the executor itself applies no admission bound. A large parallel plan can therefore start an input-proportional number of action handlers and their downstream work simultaneously.

**Observed Behavior**

At the extracted gather call, all 64 controlled step awaitables were admitted before any was released. Peak active tasks and pending child tasks both reached 64. The reproduction uses finite local awaitables and contacts no model provider or external service.

**Affected Version**

- Package: `elizaos` 2.0.0a5
- Python: CPython 3.11.0rc1
- File: `elizaos/advanced_planning/planning_service.py`
- Method: `PlanningService._execute_parallel`
- Call site: line 539, `await asyncio.gather(*tasks, return_exceptions=True)`
- The canonical TypeScript implementation has the equivalent all-step `Promise.all` behavior in `packages/core/src/features/advanced-planning/services/planning-service.ts`.

**Reproduction**

Run the PoC below from its material directory. The shared `_site_harness` extracts the exact gather expression from the 2.0.0a5 source and supplies 64 finite local awaitables held at a barrier so task admission can be measured before completion.

**PoC Source Code**

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
from pathlib import Path
import sys

POC_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
sys.path.insert(0, str(POC_ROOT))
from _site_harness import run

REPO_ROOT = next(
    parent
    for parent in Path(__file__).resolve().parents
    if (parent / "artifacts").is_dir()
)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    raise SystemExit(run(Path(__file__).with_name("inputs") / "sites.json", Path(__file__).with_name("result.json"), REPO_ROOT))
```

Coordination: discussion #30659. CLAIMING: local ROOT calendar delete-title handoff; separate from VPS date-resolution work.17:T466,## Defect

CacheService.getBackendPreference recognizes native-redis and redis-native aliases but omits the canonical redis value. CACHE_BACKEND=redis therefore resolves to auto. In a Cloudflare Worker with both CACHE_KV and REDIS_URL configured, auto prefers KV, so explicit Redis selection is ignored.

Source confirmed at badba45ec31e7fef7b44a2e49b702a2ad99f45be in packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/cache/client.ts. This is source evidence, not a claim about current deployed configuration. Existing Worker selection tests cover auto and redis-rest but not canonical redis.

## Acceptance

- Canonical redis and existing aliases select the Redis adapter.
- A regression executes cache operations through controlled adapter transports, showing explicit Redis does not access KV even when both are configured.
- Auto continues to prefer Worker KV and redis-rest keeps its explicit behavior.
- No secret, deployment, backend configuration, or database mutation is part of this fix.

Related latency objective: #30715. This selector repair does not itself provide single-call inference admission or establish a speed improvement.
18:T958,## Problem

The staging latency certification collector cannot explain a telemetry API denial. Run33998668027 completed paired provider calls but failed with only `Cloudflare trace telemetry returned HTTP 403`. The collector writes the response privately, then cleanup removes it; its public-safe error loses the endpoint, numeric Cloudflare error code, and documentation guidance.

This leaves the operator unable to distinguish an absent permission from account scope or token restrictions. It does not prove which authorization condition failed.

## Scope

Improve only sanitized diagnostics for the Workers telemetry `keys` and `query` request boundaries in `packages/cloud/scripts/cloudflare-inference-trace-evidence.mjs` and their certification caller. Related full-flow tracking: #16079.

## Acceptance

- Preserve a bounded endpoint enum, HTTP status, validated numeric Cloudflare error codes, and only known official documentation links from non-success telemetry responses.
- Never export arbitrary upstream messages, raw bodies, headers, credentials, account IDs, query parameters, or attacker-controlled URLs.
- Keep failure semantics and private-response cleanup intact; do not retry authorization failures as success or claim permission failure from status alone.
- Exercise real collector/certification error propagation with denied HTTP responses, malformed bodies, and adversarial fields; inspect the emitted error/artifact and cleanup result.
- No token or infrastructure permission changes, workflow dispatch, deployment, or new privileged request are part of this change.

## Evidence and references

- Historical run: https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/33998668027
- Both documented POST endpoints require Workers Observability Write: https://developers.cloudflare.com/api/resources/workers/subresources/observability/subresources/telemetry/methods/keys/ and https://developers.cloudflare.com/api/resources/workers/subresources/observability/subresources/telemetry/methods/query/
- Cloudflare contextual403 guidance: https://developers.cloudflare.com/changelog/post/2026-08-20-contextual-403s/

UI and live-model evidence are not applicable to this bounded operator-diagnostic change; no provider dispatch or model payload changes. Existing hosted latency evidence identifies the affected operational boundary, not a successful new live authorization attempt.

The production Worker configuration enables inference auth caching but disables its required strong-revocation boundary. Warm inference can therefore retain authoritative authorization work. The documented hot path also describes multiple serial admission calls, while current source has partially fused operations; existing isolated benchmarks do not prove a complete local Worker-to-live-Cerebras request has zero critical-path database calls.

## Scope and acceptance

- Exercise the real local Cloudflare Worker against Cerebras using synthetic inputs and isolated local account state. Do not call OpenAI or Anthropic services.
- Instrument complete streaming and non-streaming inference requests: cache operations, admission RPCs, database activity, provider dispatch, first useful text, completion, and deferred accounting.
- Reach one synchronous admission/cache decision with no database work before provider dispatch or blocking response completion; document any unmet portion explicitly. Preserve credential revocation, tenant isolation, concurrent admission correctness and recovery rather than silently dropping guards.
- Exercise cold/missing/invalid/stale cache, revoked credentials, concurrent requests, provider failures, cancellation and settlement failures with real Worker storage where applicable.
- Verify accounting and cache updates after the response, without cache readback.
- Run owning package and repository gates, deploy through the approved workflow, and validate the exact production revision with live Cerebras responses and request traces.

Existing related implementation: #30094 / #30096 and #30765. This scope follows the full request through the Worker, rather than treating an auth-only empty-body probe as successful inference.
1a:T6a0,# Subscription funding retries reallocate already-reserved funds

The inference billing boundary calls SubscriptionFundingService.reserve with a stable logical operation identifier. The service locks the organization but recomputes the allowance/purchased split before checking an existing reservation. Reserving three dollars from four dollars of available allowance leaves one dollar; an exact retry then attempts one dollar of allowance plus two dollars of purchased credits instead of returning the original allocation.

Confirmed in an isolated real PostgreSQL integration run: unchanged baseline passed 3 tests / 24 assertions. A retry regression fails with SUBSCRIPTION_FUNDING_INSUFFICIENT after the original reservation succeeds, with no purchased balance. No deployed data was used. This also introduces unnecessary reads and attempted mutation work on retries.

Acceptance:
- Under the existing organization lock, resolve exact logical-operation replay before current-period lookup or purchased debit.
- Return the original reservation, allocation, timestamps and expiry without renewing capacity or counting another debit.
- Reject conflicting amount/operation and preserve purchased-only explicit-expiry mismatch behavior.
- Relative TTL replay preserves the original expiry rather than recomputing it.
- Cover exact replay after capacity decreases, conflict rollback, and persisted allocation/ledger invariants using real PostgreSQL.
- Preserve existing terminal replay semantics; replay is not fresh provider dispatch authorization.

Related latency scope: #30715. This fix does not remove the subscriber path's synchronous SQL authority or establish a deployed speed improvement.

1c:Te33,## Observed failure

On the rebuilt and installed Android APK at `a4cfe886cd8ccf84f76dfcfa89d552a805d09bbd` (APK SHA-256 `db74601d662b980bd0ce70c1b49af99ce06dae7d83d07b3fe152b69ebf9fd75f`), the canonical `test:sim:local-chat:android:live` passed authenticated process stability and bionic-host readiness, then failed the real streamed conversation turn:

- Input: `Reply with exactly these four words: android smoke model works.`
- Actual reply: `I am ready to assist you with your queries. How can I help you today?`
- Required reply: `android smoke model works`

The full smoke exited 1 after 188.71 seconds. This is not a full-chat acceptance pass.

## Confirmed source mismatch

`packages/agent/src/api/chat-routes.ts:buildAndroidLocalDirectChatPrompt` hardcodes Gemma `<start_of_turn>` markers. Independently, `plugins/plugin-capacitor-bridge/src/mobile-device-bridge-bootstrap.ts:makeGenerateHandler` unconditionally uses `buildGemmaBionicPrompt`, including converting a supplied ChatML prompt into Gemma.

The actual default smoke GGUF (`eliza-1-e2b-32k.gguf`, 1,270,808,512 bytes, SHA-256 `a511452ec932613d6b26b4fa24488fd431eb61eac69321460447d475edc221e2`) declares architecture `qwen35` and a ChatML tokenizer chat template. The model filename is not a reliable template contract. A separate real-native ChatML diagnostic is pending; the mismatch is confirmed, but whether it fully explains this reply remains to be established.

## Acceptance

- The local provider/native model owner formats complete structured messages using the actual loaded model's template. Route code does not force Gemma framing.
- Preserve all user/system/history content and ordered roles; unsupported template or metadata failures must be explicit rather than silently selecting another model family.
- Exercise Gemma and ChatML consumer behavior with realistic regression tests, including caller-supplied control-marker text and model changes.
- Rebuild/install the exact revision and pass the canonical Android local-chat smoke with the pinned default artifact. Record complete model input/output and actual response readback.
- Preserve dedicated embedding behavior and chat-model state across embedding calls.

Related integration: #30604, #30692, #30759. The root verification and Android artifact build are already green; this issue records the missing consumer-visible behavior those checks did not prove.


## Diagnostic results and evidence

Both raw ChatML streaming diagnostics timed out: one with background generation active, and one after a fresh app launch with the owned agent child temporarily paused to prevent background contention. The child was resumed in `finally`; the app was then stopped for teardown without deleting data. This does not prove corrected ChatML produces the desired reply.

The full app request used native buffered `GENERATE`, whereas the diagnostic used `GENERATE_STREAM`. Separate template correctness from streaming or cold-load behavior before assigning the entire failure to formatting.

1e:T7a1,## Scope

A package review found existing failure paths that report absence or success after a required operation fails, plus redundant tests and stale package documentation. The affected consumers include workspace audits, project-registry updates, migration imports, evidence-provider requests, and DNS audit tooling.

Concrete examples: workspace glob traversal suppresses non-ENOENT filesystem errors, project upserts can replace malformed persisted registry data, evidence request deadlines stop at headers before the body is consumed, and the DNS chunk-joining test never exercises the resolver.

## Acceptance criteria

- Preserve supported missing-input and optional-platform contracts while propagating unexpected failures at their designated boundaries.
- Prove malformed persisted registry data remains unchanged after rejected mutation.
- Keep request deadlines active through response consumption and reuse existing bounded-request infrastructure where contracts match.
- Exercise real filesystem, HTTP, migration, and DNS boundaries in focused regressions; remove duplicated implementation-only assertions.
- Correct stale package docs and reduce duplicated root contribution guidance without weakening requirements.
- Produce local per-package reports listing findings, changes, verification, and remaining review limits.
- Run owning package gates and root verify; document actual infrastructure failures separately.

## Evidence plan

Logs and domain artifacts: focused command logs and real temporary filesystem/HTTP fixtures. Screenshots/audio: required if rendered UI/audio behavior is changed. Live-model trajectories: required if agent/model behavior changes; N/A for deterministic tooling and documentation-only changes.

Implementation will be split into reviewable changes; no feature removal, public compatibility deletion, or bulk catch/default removal. Security-sensitive review details are excluded from this public record.
1f:T419,WorkflowEditor does not handle rejected execution/revision list reads, and its polling catch silently retains a stale live status. Switching the selected definition also leaves old executions and revisions visible until new reads settle, without guarding late responses from the old definition.

Affected boundary: users inspecting Runs and History, or switching definitions while API reads are pending.

Acceptance:
- Loading, empty, and failed reads render distinct states with retry.
- Switching definitions clears previous detail and ignores late reads and poll responses from the prior definition.
- Failed run polling visibly reports stale status and recovers when reads succeed.
- Failed cancel/approval/restore interactions report actionable errors without unhandled rejections.
- Saving a definition is reported as successful even if the separate history refresh fails.

The specialized `renderGroundedReceiptReply` branch in `packages/core/src/runtime/planner-loop.ts` is a second evaluator implementation. Coordination: Discussion #30659; working branch nubsstableDONOTDELETE per owner instruction.25:T12b4,## Problem

#28408 broadened `keepAliveAfterComplete` to ordinary durable Smithers sessions so they can accept follow-ups. The retained ACP session becomes `ready`, while its durable owner task remains terminal. Two existing lifecycle consumers interpret that split state unsafely:

1. **The watchdog treats intentional promptable idle as stalled work.** `detectStalledSessions` excludes only terminal ACP statuses, so a retained `ready` session crosses the default three-minute threshold and receives `STALL_GRILL_PROMPT`. That creates an unsolicited model turn after completed work; because activity refreshes and the dedupe set can clear, the cycle can repeat.
2. **Capacity reclamation can stop an in-flight follow-up.** `reclaimIdleSession` also excludes only terminal ACP statuses. A follow-up changes the ACP session to `busy`, but its durable owner task is still terminal, so queue pressure can select and stop that busy session.

The merge commit [`0cc958b7`](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/commit/0cc958b7ca9cb8ecbd377c99fd3bcdd02d976ee4) is an ancestor of current `develop` [`e68ba2e8`](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/commit/e68ba2e84ee09a0b5cb6c6a4b7e03861be295ba0). The relevant production blobs are byte-identical between them.

## Current code path

- Durable Smithers retention is enabled in [`TASKS:create`](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/e68ba2e84ee09a0b5cb6c6a4b7e03861be295ba0/plugins/plugin-agent-orchestrator/src/actions/tasks.ts#L909-L919).
- A completed retained prompt returns to [`ready`](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/e68ba2e84ee09a0b5cb6c6a4b7e03861be295ba0/plugins/plugin-agent-orchestrator/src/services/acp-service.ts#L3755-L3759).
- The watchdog filters only terminal states, then sends the grill prompt to every stale candidate ([detector](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/e68ba2e84ee09a0b5cb6c6a4b7e03861be295ba0/plugins/plugin-agent-orchestrator/src/services/task-watchdog-service.ts#L62-L77), [dispatch](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/e68ba2e84ee09a0b5cb6c6a4b7e03861be295ba0/plugins/plugin-agent-orchestrator/src/services/task-watchdog-service.ts#L272-L300)).
- A follow-up becomes [`busy`](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/e68ba2e84ee09a0b5cb6c6a4b7e03861be295ba0/plugins/plugin-agent-orchestrator/src/services/acp-service.ts#L2498-L2516), but the reclaimer still accepts every nonterminal ACP state and stops the selected session ([reclaimer](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/e68ba2e84ee09a0b5cb6c6a4b7e03861be295ba0/plugins/plugin-agent-orchestrator/src/services/orchestrator-task-service.ts#L7274-L7313)).

I added two disposable desired-behavior probes at the exact merged PR head and ran them with the existing admission suite:

```text

Expected: []
Received: [{ "id": "retained-ready", "idleMs": 180000 }]

Expected session status: "busy"
Received session status: "stopped"
```

The first probe passes one `ready` session idle for exactly `180_000` ms to `detectStalledSessions` and expects no stalled work. The second uses the existing admission integration harness: spawn task A at cap 1, mark A terminal, mark its retained session `busy` to model a follow-up, queue task B, and assert A remains busy. Both assertions fail against the merged behavior.

The later live-smoke change in #28387 covers a happy-path sequential follow-up, but it does not wait through the watchdog threshold or create queue pressure while the follow-up is busy.

## Acceptance criteria

- Retained promptable-idle sessions do not enter the stalled-worker watchdog or receive unsolicited grill prompts.
- The watchdog still detects genuinely in-flight work that has stopped making progress.
- Capacity reclamation can reclaim genuinely idle retained sessions, but never `busy`/prompt-in-flight sessions; re-check state immediately before stopping.
- Focused tests cover the default watchdog threshold, repeated watchdog ticks, cap-1 idle reclamation, and cap-1 busy follow-up pressure.
- The retained/promptable lifecycle is represented consistently enough that ACP status and durable task terminality cannot make cleanup, follow-up, and capacity consumers disagree.

31:T17c7,## Reproduced defect

The `video.keyframes` analyzer promises a guaranteed last frame but extracts the first frame decoded about one second before EOF. Its last-image command combines `-sseof -1` with `-update 1 -frames:v 1`; the one-frame limit ends decoding before the actual terminal frame.

A real 2.2-second MP4 containing 1 second red, 1 second blue and 0.2 second lime reproduces this: the current extractor tags a blue PNG as `last`; an independent complete decode produces lime at the final video timestamp, 2.1 seconds. Two agents opened and compared the actual images. The scene-cut pass also captures lime in this example, so this is not proof that every analyzed video loses its terminal state. Subtle terminal UI changes or an exhausted scene budget cannot rely on that separate pass to satisfy the guaranteed-last contract.

Affected consumers are video ingestion and OCR/visual review of emitted keyframe artifacts. A short terminal success/error state can be represented by an earlier state.

## Implementation plan

1. Retain the independently validated FFmpeg 9 compatibility repair (`-fps_mode vfr` replacing removed `-vsync`).
2. Decode through EOF with passthrough frame timing and no one-frame limit. Retain a near-EOF seek for normal recordings, writing a unique temporary candidate. If that successful seek yields no video frame (for example audio outlasts video), retry without seeking. Atomically promote only a newly produced candidate; never accept stale or partial output. Avoid average-frame-rate arithmetic and whole-video reverse buffering.
3. Share the existing 60-second terminal-pass deadline across both attempts and propagate process failures. Measure decode cost on an existing recording and a longer deterministic fixture; do not silently emit an earlier frame when extraction fails.
4. Add behavioral regressions using real video bytes: short terminal state, sub-second/single-frame video and a sparse/VFR tail. Inspect decoded pixel values of both the extractor result and the analyzer-emitted artifact; demonstrate the terminal-state case fails before the change.
5. Run on bundled FFmpeg 6 and installed FFmpeg 9, the full evidence package suite, typecheck/lint and root verification. Publish the actual source video, before/after images and validation receipts in the PR.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] The artifact tagged `last` matches the actual final decoded video frame in the cases above.
- [ ] First-frame and scene-cut behavior still works on FFmpeg 6 and 9.
- [ ] Missing/broken tools and extraction failures remain explicit.
- [ ] Full package and repository checks pass, and an independent reviewer inspects the generated images.

Discovered during Project 19 / #23110 evidence validation. This is a concrete prerequisite defect, not an inventory-only test request. The larger parent issue remains open.

## Implementation and adversarial review checkpoint

The initial complete-decode implementation corrected the pixels but encoded every frame: its terminal pass took 32.86 seconds on a deterministic 120-second 4K/30fps recording, compared with 139 ms for the old incorrect one-frame pass. The revised seek-through-EOF implementation with no-output fallback completed the **entire three-pass extractor** on the same fixture in 12.18 seconds. These timings cover different measured scopes and are not a direct end-to-end speedup ratio.

The new terminal-state pixel test failed before the repair. Current focused tests pass on both bundled FFmpeg 6 and installed FFmpeg 9: 2 files, 10 tests. An actual audio-tail fixture independently demonstrates that the seek produces no image before testing successful fallback. Package typecheck and lint pass. Final independent review, full package suite and updated root verification are still required; the earlier root pass covered only the separate one-line FFmpeg 9 compatibility change.

37:T8cc,## Scope

Payment suspension currently returns without stopping compute when `prepareSuspendBackupGate` cannot establish a backup. Bypassing that refusal is unsafe: Docker `stopForReplacement` removes the container and releases capacity, and node retirement excludes stopped/error agent rows. The sole local state copy can consequently lose both container and host protection.

Implement state-preserving payment suspension using the existing lifecycle jobs, stop intents and backup authority. This is independent of owner inactivity and does not authorize retention expiry.

## Acceptance Criteria

- Unfunded agents with failed checkpoint capture stop executing without container removal, mount deletion, secret loss, or automatic host retirement.
- Persist exact node/container/state retention authority before provider mutation; lost responses and worker crashes retain an actionable reconciliation record.
- Disable automatic container restart and confirm the exact immutable container is stopped; missing/unreachable resources remain explicit unresolved states.
- Recheck primary funding and lifecycle ownership before effects; manual work, deletion and newer generations win.
- Retried checkpoint capture runs without unrestricted agent side effects; retirement and orphan cleanup preserve the retained resource until verified state transfer or authorized deletion.
- Renewed funding resumes the same logical agent and retained state exactly once, including after host/worker failure; do not fresh boot around a missing backup.
- Test payment/backup/provider/lease races through real service and database boundaries; live isolated provider drills verify process state, mounts, restart policy, retained node and state continuity before rollout.

No UI or model behavior changes: screenshots, video and live model trajectories are N/A. Required evidence: owning tests/typecheck/lint, root verification, migration checks, structured lifecycle logs, primary DB receipts, Docker readback and state continuity. Local fixtures do not replace live provider evidence.

The persisted note title contains the entire line and its body is empty, contrary to the final confirmation's claim of a separate title and body. - Notes registers only its parent action. It does not use existing `promoteSubactionsToActions`, unlike the repaired Memory path.

Reuse existing child promotion and requiredForSubactions. Preserve legacy direct-handler aliases, owner gates and one NotesService mutation path. Provide explicit structured missing-parameter metadata rather than changing global prose regex. Clarify lossless title/body construction at the declared tool boundary. No new note store, parsing fast path or preset final reply.

3c:T2732,<!--
DRAFT ONLY — NOT FILED. Prepared 2026-09-06.
Repo:     elizaOS/eliza
Title:    extractAndParseJSONObjectFromText matches any ``` in text, so valid JSON fails
Template: .github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/bug_report.md (adds label: bug)
Tree:     /Users/ambujupadhyay/Developer/eliza @ develop 36181c29b2a45427b1b087f48b1272f23a67a231
This file is the issue BODY only, so `gh issue create --body-file` can use it verbatim.
-->

**Describe the bug**

`packages/core/src/utils/json-llm.ts:11` (develop, `36181c29b2a45427b1b087f48b1272f23a67a231`) defines:

```ts
const jsonBlockPattern = /```(?:json|json5)?\s*\r?\n?([\s\S]*?)\r?\n?```/i;
```

It is applied at `json-llm.ts:29` unconditionally, before any attempt to parse the whole text:

```ts
const match = text.match(jsonBlockPattern);
const textToParse = match ? match[1].trim() : text.trim();
```

Two properties of that pattern combine to reject input that is valid JSON:

1. It is not anchored, so the first ``` ``` ``` found anywhere wins — including one that sits inside a JSON string value, where it is data rather than a fence.
2. The body is lazy (`[\s\S]*?`), so when there is a real fence around the JSON, the capture ends at the first ``` ``` ``` inside the fenced content rather than at the closing fence.

So for `{"text":"Run ```bun test``` first","ok":true}` the capture is the bare word `bun test`, `JSON5.parse` throws, and the public wrapper `parseJSONObjectFromText` (`packages/core/src/utils.ts:980`) turns that into `null`.

Two other parsers in this repo return the object for the same input, because they use the anchored whole-value helper `unwrapWholeCodeFence` (`packages/core/src/utils/code-fence.ts:3`): `packages/core/src/runtime/json-output.ts:17` and `packages/core/src/actions/json-model-output.ts:17`. `json-llm.ts` is the copy that does not.

`extractAndParseJSONObjectFromText` is re-exported at `packages/core/src/utils.ts:1418`, and its wrapper `parseJSONObjectFromText` is called from about a dozen non-test sites, including `features/basic-capabilities/actions/reply.ts:392`, `features/basic-capabilities/index.ts:716`, `runtime.ts:10588`, `services/message.ts:13228`, `media/image-description-cache.ts:39`, the three `features/trust/actions/*` actions, and `packages/agent/src/actions/extract-params.ts`.

**To Reproduce**

Drop this file in as `packages/core/src/utils/repro-fence.test.ts`:

````ts
import { describe, it } from "vitest";
import { extractAndParseJSONObjectFromText } from "./json-llm.ts";
import { parseJSONObjectFromText } from "../utils.ts";
import { parseJsonObject } from "../runtime/json-output.ts";

const show = (label: string, fn: () => unknown) => {
  try { console.log(label, JSON.stringify(fn())); }
  catch (e) { console.log(label, "THREW:", (e as Error).message, "| cause:", String((e as Error).cause)); }
};

describe("json-llm fence", () => {
  it("A: fence inside a JSON string value", () => {
    const text = '{"text":"Run ```bun test``` first","ok":true}';
    console.log("input:", text);
    console.log("JSON.parse sanity:", JSON.stringify(JSON.parse(text)));
    show("extractAndParseJSONObjectFromText:", () => extractAndParseJSONObjectFromText(text));
    show("parseJSONObjectFromText (public):", () => parseJSONObjectFromText(text));
    show("runtime/json-output parseJsonObject:", () => parseJsonObject(text));
    console.log("regex capture:", JSON.stringify(text.match(/```(?:json|json5)?\s*\r?\n?([\s\S]*?)\r?\n?```/i)?.[1]));
  });

  it("B: prose + ```json fence, code block inside the text field", () => {
    const text = 'Sure:\n```json\n'
      + JSON.stringify({ thought: "answer with the command", text: "Run this:\n```bash\nbun test\n```" })
      + '\n```\nHope that helps!';
    console.log("input:", JSON.stringify(text));
    show("parseJSONObjectFromText (public):", () => parseJSONObjectFromText(text));
    show("runtime/json-output parseJsonObject:", () => parseJsonObject(text));
    console.log("regex capture:", JSON.stringify(text.match(/```(?:json|json5)?\s*\r?\n?([\s\S]*?)\r?\n?```/i)?.[1]));
  });

  it("C: controls, same shapes with no inner fences", () => {
    show("A-control:", () => parseJSONObjectFromText('{"text":"Run bun test first","ok":true}'));
    show("B-control:", () => parseJSONObjectFromText('Sure:\n```json\n{"thought":"t","text":"Run bun test"}\n```\nHope that helps!'));
  });
});
````

Run it:

```
cd packages/core && bunx vitest run --silent=false --reporter=verbose src/utils/repro-fence.test.ts
```

Observed output (vitest 4.1.10, three tests pass — they only log):

````
stdout | src/utils/repro-fence.test.ts > json-llm fence > A: fence inside a JSON string value
input: {"text":"Run ```bun test``` first","ok":true}
JSON.parse sanity: {"text":"Run ```bun test``` first","ok":true}
extractAndParseJSONObjectFromText: THREW: Failed to parse invalid JSON | cause: SyntaxError: JSON5: invalid character 'b' at 1:1
parseJSONObjectFromText (public): null
runtime/json-output parseJsonObject: {"text":"Run ```bun test``` first","ok":true}
regex capture: "bun test"

stdout | src/utils/repro-fence.test.ts > json-llm fence > B: prose + ```json fence, code block inside the text field
input: "Sure:\n```json\n{\"thought\":\"answer with the command\",\"text\":\"Run this:\\n```bash\\nbun test\\n```\"}\n```\nHope that helps!"
parseJSONObjectFromText (public): null
runtime/json-output parseJsonObject: {"thought":"answer with the command","text":"Run this:\n```bash\nbun test\n```"}
regex capture: "{\"thought\":\"answer with the command\",\"text\":\"Run this:\\n"

stdout | src/utils/repro-fence.test.ts > json-llm fence > C: controls, same shapes with no inner fences
A-control: {"text":"Run bun test first","ok":true}
B-control: {"thought":"t","text":"Run bun test"}
````

Case A is the input JSON.parse itself accepts. Case B is the same envelope `reply.ts` receives, a `JSON.stringify`d `{thought, text}` where `text` holds a fenced snippet. The controls are the identical shapes with the inner fences removed, and both parse.

Note that single-backtick inline code does not trigger this — only a run of three backticks.

**Expected behavior**

`extractAndParseJSONObjectFromText` returns the object, and `parseJSONObjectFromText` returns it rather than `null`:

- Case A: `{"text":"Run ```bun test``` first","ok":true}`
- Case B: `{"thought":"answer with the command","text":"Run this:\n```bash\nbun test\n```"}`

That is what `runtime/json-output.ts` and `actions/json-model-output.ts` already return for the same strings.

Actual: `extractAndParseJSONObjectFromText` throws `Failed to parse invalid JSON` (cause `SyntaxError: JSON5: invalid character 'b' at 1:1` for case A, `JSON5: invalid end of input` for the whole-value-fenced form), and `parseJSONObjectFromText` returns `null`.

**Screenshots / recording of the wrong behavior (required for anything visible)**

N/A - no UI surface; the defect is in a core parsing helper and the failure is the console output quoted above.

**Evidence / reproduction proof**

- [ ] MP4 recording or JPG screenshots of the broken behavior. — `N/A - not visible in the UI; the wrong behavior is the parser return value, captured as console output under "To Reproduce".`
- [x] Backend logs (`[ClassName] ...`) and frontend console/network logs when relevant (wrap long output in a `<details>` block). — the verbatim vitest stdout is quoted above.
- [ ] Real-LLM trajectory when the bug involves agent/action/prompt/model behavior. — `N/A - the reproduction is deterministic and needs no model: the failing inputs are fixed strings, given in full above.`
- [ ] Domain artifacts when relevant (DB rows, memories, scheduled tasks, generated files, wallet/on-chain output). — `N/A - no persisted artifacts involved; the defect is confined to the parsing helper.`

**Additional context**

Why it matters: any model reply that carries a markdown code block inside a JSON string field is unparseable through this path, which is common whenever an agent answers with a shell or code snippet. At `features/basic-capabilities/actions/reply.ts:392-402` the `null` gives `parsedText = ""`, and because the raw response does not start with `<` the code falls through to `rawText`, so the whole `{"thought":...,"text":...}` envelope is used as the reply text, escaped `\n` and internal thought included.

Proposed fix direction, tested locally against the eight assertions in `packages/core/src/utils/json-llm.test.ts` plus the cases above:

1. Try `JSON5.parse` on the whole trimmed text first, and fall back to fence extraction only if that fails or yields a non-object.
2. Anchor the fence pattern to a line-start fence with a greedy body, e.g. `/^```(?:json|json5)?[ \t]*\r?\n([\s\S]*)\r?\n```[ \t]*$/im`. A valid JSON payload cannot contain a literal newline inside a string, so a fence that opens at the start of a line is never string content.

Together those return the expected object for cases A and B and for the whole-value-fenced form, and leave all eight existing assertions passing, including the 1.1M-character value case.

One thing worth flagging for whoever picks this up: routing `json-llm.ts` through `unwrapWholeCodeFence` alone is not sufficient, and would break `json-llm.test.ts:30` ("extracts JSON from a ```json fenced block surrounded by prose"). That helper only strips a fence wrapping the entire value, so prose-surrounded input goes to `JSON5.parse` unchanged and throws. `runtime/json-output.ts` survives that case through its separate `extractJsonObjects` brace scan, not through the helper. Step 1 above preserves the prose-extraction behaviour.

Also related: open PR #30536 edits this same regex literal for a different defect in the `(?:json|json5)?` alternation, so whichever lands first will want a rebase.

I am happy to open a PR with the change and a regression test covering these cases once a maintainer confirms the direction — in particular whether to fix `json-llm.ts` in place as above, or to consolidate the three parsers onto one shared implementation.
3d:T1e24,<!-- Filed from .github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/bug_report.md -->

**Describe the bug**

`convertMarkdownToTelegram()` in `plugins/plugin-telegram/src/utils.ts` converts a markdown
heading whose text is already bold into a string with interleaved `*` delimiters, so the
heading's emphasis is either dropped or applied to the wrong span.

`# **Summary**` converts to `**Summary**`, and `## **Bold** header` converts to
`**Bold* header*`.

Mechanism, at commit `36181c29b2` (`develop`):

- Step 4 (`utils.ts:122-128`) rewrites `**Summary**` to the stored replacement `*Summary*` and
  leaves a NUL sentinel in the text.
- Step 7, the header rule (`utils.ts:176-188`), then sees a header whose content is that
  sentinel and wraps the whole line in another bold pair:

  ```ts
  // plugins/plugin-telegram/src/utils.ts:185
  const formatted = `*${escapePlainText(trimmed)}*`;
  ```

  `escapePlainText` does not touch the sentinel, so it survives.
- The sentinel resolution loop at the end of the function splices `*Summary*` back inside that
  outer pair, producing `**Summary**`.

Only bold collides this way. Header emphasis and markdown bold both resolve to a single `*`;
every other token uses a distinct delimiter (`_`, `~`, `` ` ``, `[]()`) and nests correctly.

The comment three lines above the wrap (`utils.ts:180-182`) records that `**` is the sequence
Telegram rejects as "can't find end of bold entity" (HTTP 400), which is why the empty-header
case returns `""`. The bold-heading case re-emits that sequence. I have not called the Telegram
API to confirm the response code, so I am citing the file's own note rather than an observed
400; the delimiter interleaving below is reproducible offline and is the part I can show.

**To Reproduce**

Save this as `plugins/plugin-telegram/src/repro-bold-heading.test.ts`:

```ts
import { describe, it } from "vitest";
import { convertMarkdownToTelegram } from "./utils";

describe("bold heading -> MarkdownV2", () => {
  it("prints conversions", () => {
    const inputs = [
      "# **Summary**",
      "## **Bold** header",
      "# **A** and **B**",
      "**Bold** header",        // control: bold outside a heading
      "## *Italic* header",     // control: a different entity
      "# Header with `code`",   // control: pinned by utils.test.ts:147-155
      "## plain header",        // control
    ];
    for (const input of inputs) {
      console.log(
        `${JSON.stringify(input)} -> ${JSON.stringify(convertMarkdownToTelegram(input))}`,
      );
    }
  });
});
```

Run:

```bash
cd plugins/plugin-telegram
bunx vitest run src/repro-bold-heading.test.ts --reporter=verbose
```

Observed output:

```
"# **Summary**"        -> "**Summary**"
"## **Bold** header"   -> "**Bold* header*"
"# **A** and **B**"    -> "**A* and *B**"
"**Bold** header"      -> "*Bold* header"
"## *Italic* header"   -> "*_Italic_ header*"
"# Header with `code`" -> "*Header with `code`*"
"## plain header"      -> "*plain header*"
```

The last four lines are controls and are correct. The first three are the bug.

**Expected behavior**

A bold heading should reach Telegram as one well-formed bold entity, matching the single-pair
shape the controls already produce:

| Input | Expected | Actual |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `# **Summary**` | `*Summary*` | `**Summary**` |
| `## **Bold** header` | `*Bold header*` | `**Bold* header*` |
| `# **A** and **B**` | `*A and B*` | `**A* and *B**` |

MarkdownV2 has no bold inside bold, so collapsing the inner emphasis into the heading's single
bold entity is the only representable rendering. That is the same shape `utils.test.ts:147-155`
already pins for a nested token: `convertMarkdownToTelegram("# Header with \`code\`")` must be
`` "*Header with `code`*" `` — one bold pair with the nested token inside it.

**Screenshots / recording of the wrong behavior (required for anything visible)**

`N/A - the failure is a string transformation in a pure exported function, and its full observed
output is pasted verbatim under "To Reproduce" above. I do not have a bot token to record the
rendered Telegram message; the rendered symptom follows from the delimiter positions shown
there. Happy to add a recording if a maintainer would like one against a live bot.`

**Evidence / reproduction proof**

- [ ] MP4 recording or JPG screenshots of the broken behavior. — `N/A - see the Screenshots section above; the observed output is included as text.`
- [x] Backend logs (`[ClassName] ...`) and frontend console/network logs when relevant (wrap long output in a `<details>` block). — the vitest stdout above is the complete observed output.
- [ ] Real-LLM trajectory when the bug involves agent/action/prompt/model behavior. — `N/A - the bug is in the outbound formatting conversion and does not depend on model behavior; the input is a literal string.`
- [ ] Domain artifacts when relevant (DB rows, memories, scheduled tasks, generated files, wallet/on-chain output). — `N/A - no persistence involved.`

<details>
<summary>Where the converted string is used</summary>

```
plugins/plugin-telegram/src/messageManager.ts:1170   const chunk = convertMarkdownToTelegram(chunks[i]);
plugins/plugin-telegram/src/messageManager.ts:1209     parse_mode: "MarkdownV2",

plugins/plugin-telegram/src/messageManager.ts:2450   const formatted = convertMarkdownToTelegram(text);
plugins/plugin-telegram/src/messageManager.ts:2458     { parse_mode: "MarkdownV2" },
```

Sending the same input through `MessageManager.sendMessageInChunks()` with the transport stubbed
puts the malformed string on the wire under `parse_mode: "MarkdownV2"`:

```
input: "# **Summary**\n\nThe deploy finished.\n\n## **Next** steps"
wire:  "**Summary**\n\nThe deploy finished\\.\n\n**Next* steps*"
parse_mode: "MarkdownV2"
```

</details>

**Additional context**

Affected line: `plugins/plugin-telegram/src/utils.ts:185`, inside the header rule at
`utils.ts:176-188`. Verified 2026-09-06 against `develop` at `36181c29b2`
(`@elizaos/plugin-telegram` 2.0.3-beta.7).

Why it matters: a heading with bold text is a common shape in model output, and
`convertMarkdownToTelegram()` is the only outbound formatting path in the plugin — every reply
sent through `sendMessageInChunks()` and every edit through `editMessage()` goes through it. The
outcome a user sees is a heading that is not bold (`# **Summary**` puts the emphasis on two
empty spans), or, for a partially bold heading, emphasis on the wrong words (`## **Bold** header`
emphasises `" header"` rather than `"Bold"`). If Telegram does reject the leading `**` as the
comment at `utils.ts:180-182` describes, `sendWithRetry`'s 400 handler matches `/entit/` and
re-sends `cleanText(chunk)`, so one bold heading anywhere in a reply would drop formatting from
the whole message.

Proposed fix direction: in the header rule, resolve `trimmed` against `replacements` before
wrapping it. If it is exactly one stored replacement that already opens and closes with `*`,
reuse that replacement as-is rather than adding a second pair; if it merely contains bold
sentinels among other text, splice their inner text in un-bolded so exactly one bold entity is
emitted for the line. That keeps the change local to `utils.ts:176-188` and follows the same
defensive style as the existing `if (!trimmed) return "";` guard, which is already there to stop
this rule emitting `**`. Regression cases would be the three rows in the table above plus the
existing controls.

Happy to open a PR with the fix and tests once a maintainer confirms this is the direction they
want — in particular whether collapsing the inner bold (rather than, say, dropping the heading's
own bold and keeping the inner span) is the preferred resolution.
3e:T1a68,**Description**

The Python `get_follow_ups_context` provider in the current `elizaos` distribution passes an entity lookup for every upcoming follow-up to `asyncio.gather` at once. The number of scheduled follow-ups therefore directly controls the number of simultaneously admitted entity queries. The TypeScript provider in the canonical repository has the equivalent complete-list `Promise.all` pattern for its unique contact IDs.

**Observed Behavior**

With 512 local follow-up records, all 512 entity queries were admitted before any was released, and peak concurrent queries reached 512. The runtime and service were finite local doubles; no database or external service was contacted.

**Affected Version**

- Package: `elizaos` 2.0.0a5 (the current PyPI release)
- Python: CPython 3.11.0rc1
- File: `elizaos/advanced_capabilities/providers/follow_ups.py`
- Function: `get_follow_ups_context`
- Confirmed source range: lines 13–36
- Canonical repository equivalent: `packages/core/src/features/advanced-capabilities/providers/followUps.ts`, complete-list `Promise.all` at lines 65–67.

**Reproduction**

Run the PoC below from its material directory with the `elizaos` 2.0.0a5 source archive available at the configured archive path. It extracts the released provider function, supplies 512 local follow-ups, and holds entity lookups at a barrier so admission can be measured before completion.

**PoC Source Code**

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Reproduce follow-up-proportional fan-out in ElizaOS 2.0.0a5."""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import ast
import asyncio
import __future__
import json
import sys
import tarfile
import tempfile
import types
from datetime import UTC, datetime, timedelta
from pathlib import Path


REPO_ROOT = next(parent for parent in Path(__file__).resolve().parents if (parent / "artifacts").is_dir())
DEFAULT_ARCHIVE = REPO_ROOT / "artifacts/review_sources/k1024/archives/elizaos-2.0.0a5.tar.gz"
DEFAULT_INPUT = Path(__file__).with_name("inputs") / "follow_ups.json"
DEFAULT_RESULT = Path(__file__).with_name("result.json")


def package_root(archive: Path, temporary: Path) -> Path:
    with tarfile.open(archive, "r:*") as bundle:
        bundle.extractall(temporary)
    return temporary / "elizaos-2.0.0a5"


def load_function(root: Path, service_type: type):
    source = root / "elizaos/advanced_capabilities/providers/follow_ups.py"
    tree = ast.parse(source.read_text(encoding="utf-8"), filename=str(source))
    nodes = [node for node in tree.body if isinstance(node, ast.AsyncFunctionDef) and node.name == "get_follow_ups_context"]
    if len(nodes) != 1:
        raise RuntimeError(f"expected one get_follow_ups_context in {source}")

    bootstrap = types.ModuleType("elizaos.bootstrap")
    services = types.ModuleType("elizaos.bootstrap.services")
    follow_up = types.ModuleType("elizaos.bootstrap.services.follow_up")
    follow_up.FollowUpService = service_type
    sys.modules[bootstrap.__name__] = bootstrap
    sys.modules[services.__name__] = services
    sys.modules[follow_up.__name__] = follow_up

    class ProviderResult:
        def __init__(self, **values: object) -> None:
            self.values = values

    namespace = {"asyncio": asyncio, "datetime": datetime, "UTC": UTC, "ProviderResult": ProviderResult}
    code = compile(ast.fix_missing_locations(ast.Module(body=nodes, type_ignores=[])), str(source), "exec", flags=__future__.annotations.compiler_flag, dont_inherit=True)
    exec(code, namespace)
    return namespace["get_follow_ups_context"]


async def reproduce(root: Path, count: int) -> dict[str, object]:
    all_started = asyncio.Event()
    release = asyncio.Event()
    started = 0
    active = 0
    peak = 0
    scheduled = (datetime.now(UTC) + timedelta(days=1)).isoformat().replace("+00:00", "Z")
    follow_ups = [types.SimpleNamespace(entity_id=i, scheduled_at=scheduled, reason="poc", priority="normal") for i in range(count)]

    class Service:
        async def get_upcoming_follow_ups(self, **_kwargs: object):
            return follow_ups

        async def get_follow_up_suggestions(self):
            return []

    class Runtime:
        def get_service(self, _name: str):
            return Service()

        async def get_entity(self, identifier: str):
            nonlocal started, active, peak
            started += 1
            active += 1
            peak = max(peak, active)
            if started == count:
                all_started.set()
            await release.wait()
            active -= 1
            return types.SimpleNamespace(name=f"entity-{identifier}")

    function = load_function(root, Service)
    operation = asyncio.create_task(function(Runtime(), object()))
    await asyncio.wait_for(all_started.wait(), timeout=2.0)
    admitted = started
    release.set()
    await asyncio.wait_for(operation, timeout=2.0)
    reproduced = admitted == count and peak == count
    return {
        "status": "reproduced" if reproduced else "not_reproduced",
        "python": sys.version.split()[0],
        "python_executable": sys.executable,
        "input_follow_ups": count,
        "entity_queries_admitted_before_release": admitted,
        "peak_concurrent_entity_queries": peak,
        "external_service_contacted": False,
        "observed_effect": "follow-up-proportional concurrent entity-query admission",
        "source": "elizaos-2.0.0a5/elizaos/advanced_capabilities/providers/follow_ups.py:13-36",
    }


def main() -> int:
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    parser.add_argument("--input", type=Path, default=DEFAULT_INPUT)
    parser.add_argument("--archive", type=Path, default=DEFAULT_ARCHIVE)
    parser.add_argument("--package-root", type=Path)
    parser.add_argument("--result", type=Path, default=DEFAULT_RESULT)
    args = parser.parse_args()
    print(f"python: {sys.version.split()[0]} ({sys.executable})")
    count = int(json.loads(args.input.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))["follow_up_count"])
    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory(prefix="aegis-poc-elizaos-") as directory:
        root = args.package_root or package_root(args.archive, Path(directory))
        result = asyncio.run(reproduce(root, count))
    args.result.write_text(json.dumps(result, indent=2) + "\n", encoding="utf-8")
    print(f"input follow-ups: {result['input_follow_ups']}")
    print(f"entity queries admitted before release: {result['entity_queries_admitted_before_release']}")
    print(f"peak concurrent entity queries: {result['peak_concurrent_entity_queries']}")
    print(f"unbounded fan-out reproduced: {'yes' if result['status'] == 'reproduced' else 'no'}")
    return 0 if result["status"] == "reproduced" else 1


if __name__ == "__main__":
    raise SystemExit(main())
```

41:Tc98,## What

`packages/core/src/connectors/privacy.ts` defines the ordered connector-account privacy scale (`owner_only < team_visible < semi_public < public`) whose header documents it as the gate for "whether an account's data may surface in providers, summaries, and public posts", with a **fail-safe default to `owner_only`** when the stored value is missing or non-canonical. None of its exported contract is owned by any test:

- `rg privacyAtLeast|getAccountPrivacy|isPrivacyLevel` across all `*test*` files returns zero tests that import `packages/core/src/connectors/privacy.ts` (the only matches are unrelated mocks — see below).
- `packages/core/src/connectors/__tests__/` contains suites for `account-manager`, `attachments`, and `oauth-role`, but **no `privacy.test.ts`**.

This is a data-exposure boundary: a rank-order regression (e.g. `PRIVACY_LEVEL_RANK` entries swapped so `team_visible >= public` compares true, or the fail-safe default flipping to `semi_public`) would silently surface `owner_only` account data to non-owner audiences.

## Who observes a regression

Live consumers importing these helpers today:

- `plugins/plugin-browser/src/routes/workspace-account-gate.ts` (L10, L147) — gates browser workspace operations on `getAccountPrivacy(account)`; a default flip exposes owner-only connector accounts (Gmail etc.) in workspace tabs.
- `packages/agent/src/api/connector-account-routes.ts` (L202 write validation, L444 read gate) — the HTTP API's account read gate.

## Why existing tests do not own it

Both consumer suites substitute their own copy instead of exercising the real core module:

- `plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/src/__tests__/privacy.test.ts` L19+ **mocks `@elizaos/core`** with a hand-copied `getAccountPrivacy` — a rank regression in core passes this suite green.
- `packages/agent/src/api/connector-account-routes.test.ts` L305 mocks `isPrivacyLevel` locally.
- `plugins/plugin-browser/src/routes/__tests__/workspace-account-gate.test.ts` exercises real gate behavior but only at `owner_only` (its happy path), never the ordered-scale or fail-safe-malformed-value contract.

## Acceptance criteria

A `packages/core/src/connectors/__tests__/privacy.test.ts` suite importing the real module that pins:

1. **Ordered comparison** — `privacyAtLeast(actual, required)` across the full rank matrix (equal levels true; each strictly-lower level vs each higher required level false; both directions sampled, not just adjacent pairs).
2. **Fail-safe default** — `getAccountPrivacy` returns `owner_only` for: missing `metadata`, missing `privacy` key, `undefined`, `null`, empty string, and non-canonical strings (e.g. `"Public"`, `" public "` with case/whitespace drift, `"everyone"`) — i.e. malformed stored data can never widen visibility.
3. **Type guard exactness** — `isPrivacyLevel` accepts exactly the four canonical strings and rejects near-misses (case variants, whitespace, `toString` of non-strings), so write-path validation (`connector-account-routes.ts` L202) cannot admit values the rank lookup silently treats as `undefined`.

Tests must assert behavior at the module boundary with real inputs — no re-asserting the `PRIVACY_LEVELS` literal array.
42:Ta80,## Summary

Reviewing #29966 surfaced an unpinned security-relevant contract: the **read-only exemption partition** layered on top of `RUNTIME_MANAGEMENT_OPERATIONS` is expressed twice, in two different ways, and enumerated by no test.

## Evidence (verified at develop `e7b7e7c905`)

- `packages/agent/src/api/runtime-management-routes.ts:24` — `READ_ONLY_OPERATIONS = new Set([...])`, a hand-written literal of `{list, inspect_ssh}`.
- `plugins/plugin-app-control/src/actions/runtime-management.ts:41` — `CONFIRMATION_REQUIRED`, *derived* by filtering `"list"` and `"inspect_ssh"` out of the shared constant.

`rg '"list"|"inspect_ssh"' --type ts` across both packages confirms neither symbol is named in any test file at this head. The route suite exercises the partition only indirectly (an `inspect_ssh` round-trip without a proposal, and the destructive-op proposal binding).

## The failure mode

Adding a read-only operation to the shared `RUNTIME_MANAGEMENT_OPERATIONS` constant updates the plugin's derived `CONFIRMATION_REQUIRED` view automatically, while leaving the route's hand-written `READ_ONLY_OPERATIONS` literal behind — the two views of "which operations skip owner approval" silently diverge, and no test fails.

## Proposed fix

Extend the contract-pinning suite added in #29966 (`packages/shared/src/contracts/runtime-management.test.ts`, or a sibling file where the plugin constant is importable) with:

1. A pin that the exempt set is exactly `{list, inspect_ssh}` at both expression sites (the route literal and the plugin's derived set), failing on any divergence.
2. Optionally, a same-PR consistency assertion that `CONFIRMATION_REQUIRED ∪ exempt == RUNTIME_MANAGEMENT_OPERATIONS` so the partition stays a true partition as operations are added.

## Regression test

The pins above are the regression tests: drop `inspect_ssh` from one site or add a new read-only op to the shared constant without updating the route literal, and the suite goes red.

43:T107b,## Summary
`defaultSpawn` in claude-cli.ts is the production spawner for BOTH the Claude and Codex CLI inference variants (codex-cli-exec.ts routes through the same seam). On every call it does `stdinFd = openSync(opts.stdinPath, "r")` (opts.stdinPath is always "/dev/null") and hands that raw fd to `spawn(..., { stdio: [stdinFd, "pipe", "pipe"] })`, but never closes it. Node/libuv dups the fd for the child during spawn; the parent's copy is leaked. There is no closeSync anywhere in the file. Each ClaudeCli.generate()/CodexCli.generate() call — i.e. every inference turn served over the SAFE/CLI route — leaks exactly one file descriptor in the host process. Under sustained agent use this monotonically climbs to the process fd limit and then every subsequent openSync/socket/file operation fails with EMFILE, taking down inference and anything else in the runtime that needs a descriptor. This is a slow-burn resource leak on the hottest path of the plugin, not a cold error branch.

## Reproduction
```
Node's stdio-fd sharing does not close the parent fd. Reproduced the exact pattern used by defaultSpawn:

/tmp/fdleak.mjs (openSync("/dev/null","r") + spawn(stdio:[fd,"pipe","pipe"], detached:true), no close, 50 iterations, counting /proc/self/fd):
  $ node fdleak.mjs
  fds before: 21 after 50 spawns: 72 delta: 51
=> ~1 leaked fd per spawn (50 spawns → +51 open descriptors).

Adding `closeSync(stdinFd)` in the child 'close'/'error' handlers (the proposed fix):
  $ node fdfix.mjs
  fds before: 21 after 50: 22 delta: 1
=> leak eliminated. Confirms both the defect and the fix.
```

## Proposed fix
In defaultSpawn (claude-cli.ts), close the parent's copy of stdinFd once it has been handed to the child. Minimal change: define `const closeStdin = () => { try { closeSync(stdinFd); } catch {} };` (import closeSync from node:fs) and call it in both terminal handlers — `child.on("error", (err) => { clearTimers(); closeStdin(); reject(err); })` and `child.on("close", (code, signal) => { clearTimers(); closeStdin(); resolve(...) })`. Also guard the case where `spawn()` throws synchronously by wrapping it so stdinFd is closed on that path too. libuv dups the fd during spawn, so closing after the child is created is safe and does not affect the child's stdin. Test plan (Linux-gated vitest, ~15 lines, real defaultSpawn, no spawn mock): resolve a trivial binary, snapshot readdirSync('/proc/self/fd').length, invoke defaultSpawn 30+ times awaiting each, snapshot again, assert the delta is small (< 5) rather than ≈ call count; a second assertion runs the pre-fix pattern inline to document the ≈1-per-call growth the fix removes.

## Evidence plan
PR attaches: (1) before/after `node` fd-count reproduction output shown above (delta 51 → delta 1); (2) the new Linux-gated vitest failing on the current code (fd delta ≈ call count) and passing after the closeSync fix; (3) `bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-cli-inference test` and typecheck output green.

`defaultSpawn` in plugins/plugin-cli-inference/src/claude-cli.ts (shared by both the Claude and Codex production inference paths via codex-cli-exec.ts) opens the required `/dev/null` stdin file descriptor with `openSync(opts.stdinPath, "r")` and passes the raw fd into `spawn(...)` stdio, but never closes the parent's copy of that fd. Node dups the fd into the child at spawn time; the parent's descriptor stays open forever. Every `ClaudeCli.generate()` / `CodexCli.exec()` call — i.e. every large-tier chat/route/synthesis inference turn when ELIZA_CHAT_VIA_CLI is enabled — leaks exactly one fd. On a long-running agent this monotonically grows the process fd table until it hits the OS limit and subsequent spawns/opens fail with EMFILE, taking down the inference route (and anything else needing an fd). This is the core inference data path, so the leak is unbounded in normal operation, not an edge case.

## Reproduction
```
Mechanism proven with a standalone reproduction that mirrors defaultSpawn's exact stdio handling (openSync + spawn with the fd, detached, no close):
  /tmp/fdtest.mjs: opens /dev/null via openSync, spawns a child with stdio:[fd,'pipe','pipe'], never closes fd; loops 20 times counting /proc/self/fd.
  Observed: "fds before: 21" -> "fds after 20 spawns: 42" (one fd leaked per call).
Control with the fix (closeSync(fd) after spawn), /tmp/fdtest2.mjs and fdtest3.mjs:
  Observed: "fds after 20 spawns (with closeSync): 22" (flat), and child stdin still functions — child reads "EOF:0" from /dev/null, so closing the parent copy immediately after spawn does not break the child's stdin.
Expected per the file's own contract ("`/dev/null` stdin is REQUIRED"): the fd is a per-call resource that must be released; observed: it is never closed, so N inference calls hold N open fds.
```

## Proposed fix
In defaultSpawn (plugins/plugin-cli-inference/src/claude-cli.ts), close the parent's copy of stdinFd once the child has inherited it. Since libuv dups the fd synchronously during spawn, add `try { closeSync(stdinFd); } catch {}` immediately after the `spawn(...)` call returns (import closeSync from node:fs). This releases the descriptor for both the success and error/timeout paths without touching the child's stdin (verified: child still sees /dev/null EOF). Scope is ~2 lines plus the import; both CLI variants share this spawner so a single fix covers claude-cli and codex-cli-exec. Test plan (>=10 real lines) added to __tests__/cli-inference.test.ts alongside the existing real-spawn "defaultSpawn timeout escalation" block: a test that snapshots readdirSync('/proc/self/fd').length, runs defaultSpawn against a trivial real child (process.execPath -e 'process.exit(0)') ~15 times with stdinPath '/dev/null', and asserts the post-run fd count has not grown by more than a small constant (proving no per-call leak). Gate the assertion on process.platform === 'linux' (procfs) or use a portable fd probe; the existing suite already spawns real children here so no new harness is required.

## Evidence plan

4d:T1495,## Summary
`defaultSpawn` in plugins/plugin-cli-inference/src/claude-cli.ts opens `/dev/null` with `openSync(opts.stdinPath, "r")` (line 94) and passes the numeric fd to `spawn` as `stdio[0]` (line 105), but never closes the PARENT process's copy of that descriptor. When a raw integer fd is handed to child_process stdio, Node/Bun does not close the parent's fd — the caller owns it. So every `ClaudeCli.generate()` / `CodexCli.generate()` call (the cold `claude`/`codex` CLI inference backends, both routed through this single `defaultSpawn`) leaks exactly one open descriptor for the lifetime of the agent process. A long-running agent doing repeated CLI-route inference monotonically accumulates open fds until it hits the process `ulimit -n`, after which every subsequent `openSync`/`spawn` (and other I/O across the whole process) fails with EMFILE — breaking not just inference but all file/socket operations. This is a teardown/resource-lifecycle defect on a real production path, not input validation.

## Reproduction
```
The leak is in claude-cli.ts lines 88-106: `stdinFd = openSync(opts.stdinPath, "r")`, then `spawn(argv[0], argv.slice(1), { stdio: [stdinFd, "pipe", "pipe"], detached: true })`, and `stdinFd` is never closed in `child.on("close")`/`"error"`/anywhere (grep confirms: the only `openSync`/`stdinFd` references are lines 2,90,94,105; no `closeSync` exists in the package). Faithful minimal repro of those exact lines (Linux, /proc fd count):
  cat > /tmp/fdtest.mjs <<'EOF'
  import { spawn } from "node:child_process";
  import { openSync, readdirSync } from "node:fs";
  const run = () => new Promise((res,rej)=>{ const fd=openSync("/dev/null","r"); const c=spawn("true",[],{stdio:[fd,"pipe","pipe"],detached:true}); c.on("error",rej); c.on("close",()=>res()); });
  const before = readdirSync(`/proc/${process.pid}/fd`).length;
  for (let i=0;i<50;i++) await run();
  await new Promise(r=>setTimeout(r,200));
  console.log("delta", readdirSync(`/proc/${process.pid}/fd`).length - before);
  EOF
  node /tmp/fdtest.mjs
Observed: `open fds before: 21 after 50 spawns: 72 delta: 51` (one leaked fd per call). Expected: bounded fd count (~0 delta). Adding `child.on("spawn", () => closeSync(fd))` (plus close on error/close) yields `delta 1`, and a `cat` child with the same closed-in-parent /dev/null stdin still exits code 0 at EOF (stdin inheritance intact).
```

## Proposed fix
In `defaultSpawn` (claude-cli.ts), close the parent's copy of `stdinFd` once the child has inherited it. Import `closeSync` from node:fs; add an idempotent `closeStdin()` guard that calls `closeSync(stdinFd)` and invoke it on the child's `"spawn"` event and again (guarded) in the existing `child.on("error")` and `child.on("close")` handlers, plus in the `openSync`-succeeded-but-`spawn`-threw path. This is ~8 changed lines, fully local to one function; the codex variant needs no change because it shares this spawner. Test plan (new src/__tests__/default-spawn-fd.test.ts, >=10 lines of real assertions): (1) capture `readdirSync('/proc/'+process.pid+'/fd').length`, call the exported `defaultSpawn(['true'], {cwd: tmpdir, env: {}, timeoutMs: 5000, stdinPath: '/dev/null'})` (or `[process.execPath, '-e', '']`) 40 times awaiting each, then assert the post delta is small/bounded (e.g. < 5) — this fails on current develop (delta ~40) and passes after the fix; (2) assert a `defaultSpawn` run whose child reads stdin still receives EOF (resolves, non-hanging) so the close does not break stdin inheritance; (3) assert the returned SpawnResult still has code 0. Guarded on `process.platform === 'linux'` for the /proc probe (repo CI is Linux) with a closeSync spy fallback assertion for portability.

## Evidence plan
The PR will attach: (a) the failing-then-passing test output for the new fd-leak test (before-fix delta vs after-fix bounded delta); (b) a raw `/proc/self/fd` count before/after 50 real `defaultSpawn` invocations on current develop vs patched, demonstrating leak (delta ~51 → ~1); (c) the stdin-inheritance sanity run showing a child still reads /dev/null EOF and exits 0 after the parent fd is closed; (d) `bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-cli-inference test` and typecheck/lint green.

In the browser fallback CameraWeb (plugins/plugin-native-camera/src/web.ts), startRecording() opens a separate microphone stream via getUserMedia({audio:true}) and merges its audio tracks into a new MediaStream used for the MediaRecorder. Those audio tracks are never stopped: stopRecording() only stops the MediaRecorder and builds the blob, and stopPreview() only stops this.mediaStream (which holds the video tracks). This violates the resource-teardown contract the rest of the class follows for the video stream.

```

Sequence: new CameraWeb(); startPreview({element}); startRecording({audio:true}); stopRecording(); stopPreview(); then expect(audioTrack.stop).toHaveBeenCalled().
Command: cd plugins/plugin-native-camera && node_modules/.bin/vitest run --config vitest.config.ts src/leak.probe.test.ts
Observed: FAIL — "expected vi.fn() to be called at least once"; the audio track's stop() is never invoked. Expected: after teardown the microphone track is stopped. (Confirmed by code: no getAudioTracks()/audioStream.stop path exists anywhere in web.ts.)
```

## Proposed fix
Persist the microphone stream (or its audio tracks) on a private field when startRecording opens it, and stop those tracks in stopRecording()'s onstop handler (and defensively in stopPreview) after the blob is assembled — mirroring the existing this.mediaStream.getTracks().forEach(track => track.stop()) teardown. Keep the recorder's merged stream but ensure the extra audio tracks are torn down exactly once. Test plan (>=10 lines): a jsdom vitest spec asserting (1) the audio track stop() is called after stopRecording, (2) it is not called before, (3) a second startRecording/stopRecording cycle stops the newly-opened audio track, and (4) stopPreview during an active recording also stops audio tracks. Harness mocks getUserMedia/MediaRecorder/MediaStream as in the probe.

## Evidence plan
The PR attaches the failing-before/passing-after vitest run for the new spec plus the existing web.test.ts suite, and a short note showing the code path (startRecording audio getUserMedia vs. stopPreview only stopping this.mediaStream video tracks) that proves the leak is on the real user recording path.

<!-- eliza-computer-attribution:v1 {"provider":"anthropic","model":"claude-opus-4-8","client":"pi-coding-agent","skill_revision":"elizaOS/eliza@515987a8cd51a094b552714dc50e0518f376e9a7:packages/skills/skills/contribute-to-eliza"} -->4f:T899,**Is your feature request related to a problem? Please describe.**

`ContextRegistry` seeds ~39 "LifeOps personal assistant" context definitions unconditionally at boot (`packages/core/src/runtime/default-contexts.ts`, registered via `_initializeCore` → `tryRegisterMany(DEFAULT_CONTEXT_DEFINITIONS)`, `runtime.ts`). Per-turn selection (`listAvailableContextsForRole` → `ContextRegistry.listAvailable`) filters only by role gate. For a character built around a narrower domain (e.g. a single-purpose trading/social-posting agent with no email/calendar/contacts/phone/health/screen_time/subscriptions/payments/tasks/goals/todos/game/browser/files/terminal/code plugin loaded), the full catalog still renders into the Stage-1 routing prompt every turn via `formatAvailableContextsForPrompt`/`{{availableContexts}}`, burning prompt tokens on categories the agent can never act on — for a 24/7 autonomous agent this compounds daily.

There's no supported extension point to trim it: `compose_state_providers` filters a different registry (provider names), and `pre_model`/`post_model` only see the already-rendered prompt string.

**Describe the solution you'd like**

An `unregister(id)` (or `unregisterMany`) method on `ContextRegistry`, or a constructor/character-level option to seed a reduced definition set (e.g. `Character.contexts?: { exclude?: AgentContext[] }` consulted in `listAvailableContextsForRole`), so a non-LifeOps deployment can drop irrelevant contexts without patching core.

**Describe alternatives you've considered**

- Regex-stripping the rendered catalog lines out of the prompt in a `pre_model` pipeline hook — works, but fragile (breaks silently if `formatAvailableContextsForPrompt`'s line format changes) and duplicates the id list outside the registry.
- Reaching into `ContextRegistry`'s private `#definitions` field — not viable, it's a genuine private class field.

**Additional context**

Confirmed still open on `develop` as of this writing — `ContextRegistry` (`packages/core/src/runtime/context-registry.ts`) is unchanged from our vendored pin (`184a4cc8`): `register`/`registerMany`/`tryRegister`/`tryRegisterMany`/`listAvailable` only, no removal path.50:Tf8c6,Advances #23093, #23094 and #23095; also supplies the consent-bound registration prerequisite for #30638. These issues remain open. Pending hosted and live-integration acceptance.

# Behavior

Stripe terminal observations now publish lifecycle history, receipt, entitlement projection, policy generation and cancellation notice intent atomically. A failed write rolls back the whole transition. Historical event replay cannot restore older subscription authority.

Session-authorized managers can schedule and undo period-end cancellation. Commands record dispatch provenance before mutation and verify canonical Customer/subscription state before publication. Uncertain outcomes reconcile by retrieval without an automatic second mutation. Undo requires the captured schedule lineage to remain valid before dispatch. Tenant-scoped pending-command pagination exposes stale source identity and command uncertainty. Scheduling cancellation preserves current access until an effective terminal transition.

Existing-v1 paid renewal verifies the complete invoice, subscription, Customer, PaymentIntent, captured charge and catalog binding before granting allowance. Only a full captured, non-refunded, unadjusted monthly payment for an adjacent effective period can publish. Duplicate invoice/event delivery cannot grant twice. Unsupported observations retain retryable incident state.

Resource and inference admission use the same primary policy generation as billing snapshots. Cached paid state refreshes after a committed Free transition; missing or ambiguous required canonical authority is unavailable. Canonical-none non-subscriber accounts retain their existing legacy fallback. New allowance reservations and snapshot spendability share current organization/source/projection/period/grant identity checks. Denied spending is explicitly unavailable while ledger amounts remain intact; eligible exhausted allowance reports an observed zero. Historical reservations retain their settlement/refund ownership.

Generic app billing registration records owner and infrastructure-payer provenance and creates individual buyer accounts only after existing app consent. Merchant and subscription policy remain explicitly unconfigured. Cancellation notices have immutable identity, one durable attempt and distinct accepted/rejected/uncertain/unavailable outcomes. Dispatch requires server-approved per-revision recipients, content and delivery window. Missing policy does not send; uncertain SMTP acceptance does not trigger automatic resend or prove delivery.

The existing authenticated Stripe cron also recovers missed terminal observations using read-only canonical SDK requests. Durable leases, retry scheduling and complete-body deadlines bound provider work without retrying writes. Recovery publishes source/projection/generation/notice atomically and preserves webhook watermark provenance. Matching later events use a no-op only for a durably applied recovery revision with a coherent projection; ordinary webhook behavior is unchanged. Independent cron lanes retain failures and successful receipts separately.

# Risks

Large: identity, sessions, account linking and required wallet operations move into owned source. Existing database migrations, encryption settings and persisted wire identifiers are deliberately preserved. Production source cutover preserved the existing database, keys and an active session; revocation after cutover passed.

<!--
Low, medium, large. List what kind of risks and what could be affected.
-->

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Own the login SDK and identity service as `@elizaos/login`. Replace external Steward package/source dependencies across desktop, Cloud and application consumers. Export React login components and wallet providers from the `@elizaos/ui` root barrel, with lazy wallet adapters and no `/login` package subpath. Remove unused trading/DeFi routes and SDK methods, the unreferenced trade-order/provider-action evaluators, their spend tracker and venue catalog; retain authentication, linking, MFA, recovery and required wallet contracts. Fix durable initialization, grant isolation, Node package resolution and graceful service shutdown. React logout now waits for access/refresh revocation and reports retryable failures; refresh revocation uses its verified tenant context. Correct IPv6 loopback parsing for HSTS. Configure a filtered Railpack deployment and resolve the shared error class from portable source in Cloudflare. Verify the standard migration ledger before startup, scope wallet provisioning and login audit writes to their tenant, and use one session verifier backed by the existing bootstrap authority query.

## What kind of change is this?

Feature and dependency migration with authentication correctness fixes.
