# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

Read `packages/login/README.md`, then run the package and browser tests below. Production now runs the owned service. The current desktop/mobile walkthrough exercises real guest sign-in and server-backed logout. Manual provider acceptance limits are listed below.

## Detailed testing steps

Current head `ab993236dcdc2d9553273d0663688846a3188e8d`: all 614 login tests passed (2,268 assertions), including real forced-policy PostgreSQL. Full repository verification passed all 377 workspace tasks and every subsequent repository audit, with no cached tasks. The exact root verification command ran with Turbo concurrency reduced from eight to one for the shared machine; no checks were omitted. Post-rebase `bun install` and login lint passed. All four PR review comments are resolved.

The review also passed 154 scoped UI tests, 181 proxy tests, browser passkey registration/sign-in, and deployed signed-login/SDK-logout checks in staging and production. Current runtime/UI sources are identical to the attached `e828f474333d` recordings; the final commit only formats the HSTS test. The final app audit passed all 219 tests; OCR found zero broken views and zero regressions across 216 captures. The eight signed-in/signed-out Cloud captures were visually inspected across all four viewport sizes. [Hosted CI](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/34056742289) passed every check, including the affected-workspace build. GitHub reports the PR clean and mergeable.


- Login package: 614 tests passed with PostgreSQL enabled. The real database test uses a restricted runtime role with every installed tenant policy enabled and forced; it checks wallet login, persisted audit records, sessions across restarts, logout revocation and rejection of an altered migration ledger. The process test also proves graceful SIGTERM shutdown.
- Production-only frozen install: real wallet login, graceful shutdown, persisted session after restart, logout and persisted revocation passed with PostgreSQL. This was a local Bun process, not a deployed Railway service.
- Node 24: built package imports and authenticated HTTP requests passed; unauthorized keys and credential-bearing redirects are rejected.
- Browser: passkey registration and sign-in through the real SDK, server and database passed with Chromium's virtual authenticator; consumed challenges and cross-scope grants are rejected.
- Full UI suite: 1,296 files passed; 13,764 tests passed and 7 existing tests skipped.
- Desktop sidecar: 39 tests passed, including real child-process wallet provisioning and reopening persisted authority.
- Production renderer: lazy-wallet/crypto confinement gates passed. App audit: 219 tests passed with no computed broken or needs-work findings. Cloud audit: 106 passed, 1 skipped, with no computed broken or needs-work findings; the scoped capture run at `dda452da1ce6` passed 12 cases and all 20 images were reviewed. Packaged OCR found no pixel-blank captures; its transcripts and browser console/network diagnostics are attached. The app-only semantic OCR command has no policies for these Cloud routes, so the same packaged engine was run directly.
- Root verification: 377 workspace tasks and all repository audits passed on the current head after rebasing onto `63323d0f77f`. `bun install` passed with no lockfile changes.


Run `bun run --cwd packages/login test`, `bun run --cwd packages/login test:browser`, and `bun run verify`. Set `LOGIN_TEST_DATABASE_URL` to an isolated local PostgreSQL administrative database to enable the persistence integration test. Never point tests at production.

video click-through of the flow.

```bash
  bun run evidence:doctor                 (check capture tools; prints fixes for any missing)
  bun run test:e2e:record                 (general E2E recordings)
  bun run test:matrix:review              (capture into one verified evidence bundle)
  bun run evidence:review:no-open -- --bundle=evidence/runs/<run-id>
                                          (re-open the exact matrix run)
  bun run --cwd packages/app audit:app    (app + cloud UI — REQUIRED for UI changes)
```

The matrix command creates and verifies one named bundle, then passes that
exact run to the reviewer. Do not replace the `--bundle` path with raw producer
directories; `--source` is only for explicit archived/ad-hoc compatibility.

A UI-touching diff (rendered `.tsx`/`.css`/`.svg` under `packages/app`,
`packages/ui`, `apps/app`, …) MUST attach real screenshot/video/OCR artifacts —
the CI evidence gate rejects `N/A` on those rows for such diffs, label or not.
If a capture tool is missing, `evidence:doctor` prints the install command;
install it rather than marking evidence `N/A`.

54:Teb7,# Relates to

# Background

When payment-stop backup capture fails, an unpaid agent must stop consuming compute without destroying its only recoverable state. This change records the exact Docker container and host identity under the existing lifecycle lease before stopping it. It disables automatic restart, verifies the stopped container, and protects its host from ordinary retirement and orphan cleanup.

Funded and manual resume reuse that retained container. Publication requires both ingress readiness and an immutable-container health readback, followed by fresh funding, lease and host-identity checks. Failed readiness retains the recovery locator; a stale worker cannot tear down a newer owner's runtime. Manual suspension of previously retained state follows the same protection path and does not get cancelled by a positive balance.

# Risks and remaining work

This is a draft, not production-ready. Real Docker/control-plane access is unavailable, so provider transport tests do not establish live stop or recovery behavior. Protected backup handoff, pending-stop manual recovery, host-loss recovery, runtime activation attestation and destructive lifecycle interactions still need completion and live proof. Retained state remains protected after successful resume, so this change alone does not reclaim its storage or host cost.

Migration 0363 adds nullable local-state retention; prerequisite 0362 adds confirmed stop revision tracking. Generated snapshot metadata inherits broader schema drift tracked in #30788: 142 current tables are absent and 14 no-longer-exported tables remain. The two full snapshots account for most of the develop-targeted diff size; they are not new runtime machinery. Reconcile generation metadata against migration history before deployment. No database migration has been applied.

# Testing

The health checkpoint before rebase passed the complete shared suite in one run: 12,676 passed, 0 failed, 208 skipped, all 350 batches; cloud API typecheck, shared lint and root verification passed. The rebased cloud source is byte-identical to that checkpoint. After rebase and install, the retained-provider and real PGlite authority tests passed: 47 tests, 0 failures, 193 assertions. Root verification on rebased head f103e382a2aada852ee7c5b41669ffbd06349e63 exited 0 (base 9b314da5e65327702d3d02ad36684e7632ebe288).

The focused tests exercise exact-container identity, stop receipts, funding and lease races, manual ownership transfer, and ingress success with failed container health. SSH/Docker transport is substituted. They are not end-to-end evidence.

55:T1c47,# Relates to

Refs #30702. Depends on the state-preserving recovery and retirement work in #30714 (#30697, #30675); those prerequisite commits are included while that PR remains unmerged. The final commit contains the funded-resume change.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

A confirmed billing suspension now becomes an automatic recovery candidate when authoritative funding returns. The existing provisioning worker scans explicit pages, so an unpaid account cannot starve funded accounts and no browser request or particular webhook order is required. It reuses provisioning jobs and the same logical agent.

A confirmed stop revision and durable resume-job grant bind admission to the original payment suspension. Queueing, execution, provider-create intent and restored endpoint publication recheck primary-database authority. Later manual lifecycle work revokes the automatic grant. Expired workers cannot publish replacements, write stale errors or remove a successor's container; late provider receipts may only enrich their existing exact cleanup identity. Restoration must finish before publication. Funding loss during restore cleans up the exact replacement while retaining its backup; lease/grant loss retains the cleanup receipt for its current owner.

Failed, quiesced recovery jobs remain discoverable. A durable two-minute delay after terminal failure prevents fresh jobs from resetting retry backoff. First funded admission is immediate. The additive migration intentionally leaves legacy receipts without an inferred confirmed revision.

## What kind of change is this?

Lifecycle and billing bug fix with an additive database migration. No owner-inactivity suspension is introduced.

# Sync with develop

- Rebased without conflicts onto `8fea4f94c29c7712ce3caf303e3350150622d048`.
- Head: `a894c5d1daaa8b440f7f23bb72b367777b47d080`.
- Pinned Bun 1.3.14 / Node 24.15.0 installation passed after rebase.
- Post-sync `bun run verify`: passed (exit 0), including workspace checks and repository audits.

# Risks

Large operational impact: provisioning, billing admission and database migration. Most added lines are Drizzle's generated full-schema snapshot; handwritten changes reuse the current lifecycle/queue rather than introducing another scheduler. Production rollout remains blocked on live restore/cleanup receipts and verified treatment of legacy stopped agents. Do not infer current provider ownership from an old receipt or backfill it solely from a stopped database row.

# Documentation changes needed?

Production runbook must include migration-before-worker deployment, current-provider readback for legacy suspension authority, recovery rollback and fault-drill evidence. Those operational artifacts are still pending; this PR remains draft.

## Detailed testing steps

- Lose a provider response after admitted creation. Verify the late immutable container receipt enriches only the existing intent.
- Exhaust a failed job, verify no immediate replacement job, advance the durable terminal timestamps beyond the retry delay, and verify a new claim.

The test no longer mocks outbound invalidation as always successful.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Preserves complete merged evaluator schemas across native-schema and JSON-object provider paths; decodes supported complete native results; retains the existing relationship-type consumer contract and rejects conflicts. Previous live HTTP200 responses could silently omit required effects or fail identity parsing.

The real-runtime benchmark now retains complete requests, per-invocation wire identity, partial failures, actual persistence and post-delivery outcomes. It distinguishes visible reply from quiescence and adds complete replay controls. A separate real-provider/PGlite semantic harness checks builtin processor effects and isolation. Its acknowledgements and failed-booking result are controlled inputs, not live chat or a connector booking.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fixes and evidence tooling. No demonstrated production latency/cost improvement is claimed.

# Documentation changes needed?

Agent/core READMEs document complete schemas, stable prefixes, real embedding prerequisites and measurement limitations.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

Inspect native SDK→evaluator regressions and real persisted domain outputs, including the failed historical attempts. Confirm invalid completions produce errors rather than effects and complete model context is retained.

# Risks

Medium. This is a broad, net-negative consolidation across shared contracts, Cloud Worker boundaries, plugin utilities, and UI composition. The main risks are missed consumers of removed compatibility copies and edge-runtime import regressions. Public exports, dynamic manifests, package builds, real SQL drivers, Miniflare, Wrangler dry-run, UI contract graphs, and the full repository verification gate cover those boundaries.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Consolidates repeated SQL, connector credential, cloud lifecycle, cryptographic/canonical JSON, and mobile runner implementations under their existing canonical owners.
- Removes obsolete widgets, unused UI elements/layouts, and plugin-local visual implementations where `@elizaos/ui` already owns the component.
- Replaces the broad Cloud Worker Core mirror with the narrow `@elizaos/core/edge` leaf and a fail-loud documents trap.
- Refreshes the repository UI molecular inventory: 889 maintained React files, 100 recognized molecular-role exports, and 12 final dispositions.
- Adds reuse-first guidance to `CONTRIBUTING.md`, paired `AGENTS.md`/`CLAUDE.md` guides, and applicable skills so contributors search for and extend canonical owners before creating parallel code.
- Adds the two English account-switch fallbacks referenced by the newest `develop` head, which otherwise failed `check:i18n`.

## What kind of change is this?

Improvement/refactor with bug fixes and documentation updates. No intentional breaking public API change.

## Reuse research

The audit searched workspace manifests, exports, callers, dynamic view manifests, stories, package-local guides, and tests across every package/plugin group. Canonical ownership was assigned to `@elizaos/core`, `@elizaos/shared`, `@elizaos/ui`, or the owning domain plugin; duplicate copies were removed only after consumer searches and contract verification.

# Documentation changes needed?

Documentation was required and is included in `CONTRIBUTING.md`, the paired repository/package agent guides, and applicable skills.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

1. Review the reuse-first policy changes and the generated UI molecular report.
2. Inspect the narrow `@elizaos/core` raw SQL/edge exports and Cloud Worker imports.
3. Inspect representative plugin/UI migrations to canonical shared contracts and atoms.
4. Review the attached before/after capture sheets and exact-head audit reports.

5a:T321f,# Relates to

This PR delivers the candidate-data materialization boundary: authenticated record inbox → physical database/files → five-component assembly → assembly-backed PRIMARY seal.

- [x] Targets `develop`, rebased without conflicts onto `dac71fc5a613529a42f8cd686ed333e0fc693b1c`.

# Sync with develop

Seven implementation commits; no historical worktree diff was imported. The rebase added only the existing migration-reader change from develop; restore source and tests are byte-identical across that rebase.

# Risks

High-impact data integrity / concurrency boundary, kept behind explicit use of the materializing factory. No applied migration, default feature gate, routing, provider-create, billing activation or deployment configuration changes. Review lock ordering, replay semantics, private filesystem identities, physical archive handling and child-process settlement particularly carefully.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Extract the authenticated physical PGlite archive using bounded strict USTAR handling into the isolated candidate. Reject unsupported entries and malformed/truncated archives.
- Validate a disposable private copy with the actual PGlite engine; preserve the authenticated extraction for exact replay. The validation child retains the quarantine kernel lock and is reaped before cleanup.
- Assemble character, database, media, plugin state and vault under one quarantine lock; persist exact tree/receipt proofs.
- Add the explicit materializing repository adapter. Hold PRIMARY source/operation/lease/catalogue/object/candidate authority through actual Agent effects; persist ledger/seal state only after the exact acknowledgement. Reuse the existing migration/terminal-command authorities.
- Execute these effects in private one-shot Agent processes. Raw bounded frames travel on stdin; stdin stays open as liveness. Only a canonical receipt digest crosses stdout. No credentials or preload options are inherited.
- Prove the compiled worker over native Linux Docker exec, including stdin cancellation and abrupt Docker-client loss while a real database-validation child is alive.

The existing metadata-only foundation API remains compatible; it is not silently selected as fallback by the new materializing factory. This worker is a trusted private process boundary, **not an authenticated public or production Docker endpoint**.

## What kind of change is this?

Additive feature; completes the data-materialization/assembly unit of explicit restore.

# Documentation changes needed?

Updated `packages/agent/README.md` with transport version 2, liveness/cancellation ownership, native Docker test prerequisites and the distinction from production quarantine bootstrap.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

1. The real PostgreSQL candidate-execution integration test: see actual five-component materialization, transaction locks, failed acknowledgements, lost COMMIT recovery and retained output.
2. The Docker materializer test: observe a live validator PID before failure injection, then remote settlement and exact retry.
3. Archive extraction/validation and candidate assembly, then the materializing repository and private transport.

## Detailed testing steps

Pinned Bun 1.3.14 / Node 24.15.0. Run focused Agent suites from `packages/agent`:

```sh
bunx --no-install vitest run --config vitest.config.ts \
  src/services/agent-backup-restore-v3-candidate-assembly.test.ts \
  src/services/agent-backup-restore-v3-candidate-materializers.test.ts \
  src/services/agent-backup-restore-v3-candidate-fs.test.ts \
  src/services/agent-backup-restore-v3-pglite-archive.test.ts \
  src/services/agent-backup-restore-v3-candidate-records.test.ts \
  src/services/agent-backup-restore-v3-materializer-process.test.ts \
  --coverage.enabled=false
```

Native Docker proof requires the built Agent package, a local `node:24.15.0-alpine` image and a running Docker daemon. The harness resolves the image to its immutable ID; it does not pull implicitly. Set `DOCKER_CONFIG` explicitly when using a named context because Vitest isolates HOME:

```sh
AGENT_RESTORE_V3_DOCKER_TESTS=1 bunx --no-install vitest run \
  --config vitest.config.ts \
  src/services/agent-backup-restore-v3-materializer-docker.test.ts \
  --coverage.enabled=false
```

For the real PostgreSQL test, from `packages/cloud/shared`, use an owned temporary directory shared with the local Docker VM for `TMPDIR`, and select the local Docker context:

```sh
env -u APPS_TENANT_DB_TEST_DSN -u DATABASE_URL -u TEST_DATABASE_URL \
  APPS_TENANT_DB_EPHEMERAL=1 APPS_TENANT_DB_TEST_IMAGE=postgres:16-alpine \
  REQUIRE_REAL_POSTGRES_RESTORE_V3_CANDIDATE_TESTS=1 \
  bun test src/db/repositories/__tests__/agent-backup-restore-v3-candidate-execution-postgres.integration.test.ts

bun test src/db/repositories/__tests__/agent-backup-restore-v3-materialization.pglite.test.ts \
  src/db/repositories/__tests__/agent-backup-restore-v3-candidate-execution.pglite.test.ts \
  src/db/repositories/__tests__/agent-backup-restore-v3-candidate-seal-authority.pglite.test.ts
```

<details>
<summary>Inspected restoration outputs and authority behavior</summary>

The real PostgreSQL fixture dumps a physical source database and deletes its test-owned source directory, then restores all five components through actual private Agent processes. It inspects independent-connection lock/visibility behavior during effects, mismatched/lost/cancelled assembly acknowledgements, lost PostgreSQL COMMIT acknowledgement and exact sealed replay after expiry. The assembly marker's bytes and inode remain unchanged on terminal replay; exact media/state/vault bytes are read back. Querying a disposable copy of the restored database returns:

```json
[{"id":1,"fact":"amber lighthouse 20732"}]
```

The native Docker test independently dumps/deletes a physical source, interrupts its real validation descendant, retries, and queries the restored output:

```json
[{"value":"amber docker lighthouse"}]
```


The following is a consolidated report of the exact query read-backs asserted by the passing real tests (not worker stdout or an LLM transcript):

```json
{
  "postgres_repository_integration": {
    "query": "SELECT id, fact FROM restored_fact ORDER BY id",
    "rows": [{"id": 1, "fact": "amber lighthouse 20732"}]
  },
  "native_docker_materialization": {
    "query": "SELECT value FROM docker_fact",
    "rows": [{"value": "amber docker lighthouse"}]
  }
}
```

These are synthetic test facts, not user data. Database queries use disposable copies so inspection cannot mutate the retained authenticated candidate.


</details>

## Known gaps / failures

- The first native test invocation could not resolve the named Colima context inside Vitest's isolated HOME. Explicit `DOCKER_CONFIG` fixed the test environment; no filesystem authority check was weakened.
- Two initial test-only Uint8Array generic inference errors were corrected before final typecheck/tests.
- Plain `bun install` fails in the unchanged fused native-inference postinstall because this macOS host lacks the Metal Toolchain. The installer and native source are unchanged from develop. The existing `ELIZA_SKIP_FUSED_INFERENCE_SETUP=1 bun install` path completed successfully, with no lockfile change. This does not claim native inference build coverage.
- Local non-Linux tests explicitly use test-only pathname emulation; the Docker lane exercises actual Linux descriptor anchors and locks.
- Provider-stream/source authority is synthetic in these materialization integration fixtures. This is not a new full provider-crypto end-to-end proof.
- The Docker container uses a networkless idle PID1 fixture with a read-only repository mount. It is not the production image/bootstrap integration.
- The new explicit coordinator variant is integrated with actual Agent materialization, but the production caller after `container_created` is still required. Production SSH transport must retain exact container/image/node-incarnation fences and cannot infer remote settlement solely from a lost local client.
- Exact generation commit/boot, signed identity/readiness/functional receipts, funding/activation/route CAS, old-target cleanup and remaining crash/node/publication scenarios stay in #20732.
- The separately authorized staging backup → wipe → restore → boot → remembered fact → exactly one response and measured RTO drill is not done. Test durations are not production RTO.
- No activation gate enabled, no deployment, no paid resource, no merge or acceptance checkbox closure. #20726 remains NO-GO; #24594/#24601 and unrelated #29921/#30815 were not absorbed.

Package review found required data and storage failures reported as healthy defaults, duplicate transport/parsing paths, broken asynchronous publication, and stale developer guidance. This coordinated cleanup preserves existing capabilities while making those failures explicit and consolidating their owning code.

- Transport: shared full-body deadlines, streaming/cancellation/backpressure, complete remote-command output or explicit rejection, and owned-process teardown.
- UI: completed locale dictionaries reach mounted consumers, explicit retry recovers failed chunks, and playback resampling preserves duration/pitch across utterances and handoffs.
- Maintenance: remove proven redundant tests/dependencies/comments, correct paired guides, share publication inventory, honor declared public source entries, and preserve audit findings across worker restarts.

### Behavior and boundaries

- Contextual navigation uses the caller-authorized live catalog before planning, retains domain candidates, and revalidates the target at dispatch. Missing from a filtered catalog means unavailable to this caller with unknown cause; it does not establish global absence or reveal hidden metadata.
- Actor/turn constraints cover show/open, manager, window, pin, close, split and tile effects. Abort during catalog retrieval prevents dispatch. Receipt identities bind step, actor and client; failed delivery remains failure.
- Strict shortcuts accept standalone commands; compound/domain requests retain normal planning. Complete requests, selected catalogs, action results and model responses are preserved.
- Creation retries bind agent, owner, operation and complete semantic arguments. Completed retries reuse the original record; conflicting key reuse fails, and intentional copies remain distinct.
- Undated lifecycle changes and their exact audit/version receipts commit atomically. Missing, foreign or protected deletion targets cannot fall back to another title; ordinary update ambiguity remains intact.

### Remaining acceptance limits

The exact 25-pushups/3-times-daily request remains a normal-host provider-capacity failure, with recorded 177,751/139,377/181,590-token rejections against 131,072 and zero effects. No pruning or implicit catalog/history truncation was introduced. A separate fresh 267-action app-control+PA runtime also rejected 167,443 tokens with zero rows, no draft and no VIEWS dispatch. Its rejected request’s complete provider input was lost when the temporary recorder overwrote pending state during fallback; this is an explicit recorder gap, not a complete trajectory. That negative and the 258-action eight-step positive are different requests/runtimes.

Calendar provider-backed operations, complete voice-operation parity and physical microphone/default-ASR acceptance remain unproven. Bare Bun node:http disconnect does not provide the same cancellation signal as explicit UI Stop; the original transport evidence remains preserved. These limits keep the broader issues and iteration open.

Medium. Native select fields now use the existing browser-native control so agent-fill reaches the same change handler as a user selection. Existing workflow picker styles move into a typed canonical variant. The opaque half-height chat sheet avoids unnecessary backdrop filtering, and the Vault focus-loss guard is installed before paint. The workspace test resolver now prefers this checkout over a sibling checkout. Onboarding transport cleanup now releases responses whose headers arrive after a deadline or caller cancellation. Public APIs and stored data shapes remain unchanged.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Deployment validation exposed real control and harness failures. Spatial dropdowns were visible to the agent bridge but could not accept its native fill operation; they now use the canonical NativeSelect and report the current selected value. The Goals browser trajectory proves Active and Paused selections change the actual rendered goal list.

Cloud tests on Linux now execute each file in a separate process to avoid the confirmed Bun isolate stdio leak. Server CI runs the process-isolated agent suite on its own runner, with a real planner check proving that the partition retains all selected tasks exactly once. App-control tests resolve the core/errors source subpath correctly.

The repair also removes opaque-sheet GPU work, prevents test imports from resolving into another checkout, and fixes observed test lifecycle and fixture faults: key-specific Vault state, lock-generation assertions, database-clock lease expiry, ANSI-independent CLI output, native dropdown interactions, and route teardown. The sanitizer benchmark compares equal work with interleaved samples while retaining its existing limits. A Node artifact-integrity suite is executed by Node instead of being incorrectly collected by Vitest. Native-library freshness tests create a real isolated Git checkout, preserving the production guard that rejects missing source. Onboarding transport cancellation owns late response bodies; deterministic clock coverage proves cancellation without depending on a one-millisecond scheduling race.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fixes to deployment validation and the controls exercised by that validation.

5e:T98b,## Summary

Stacks on #28936 and implements the safe metering/control-plane portion of #25518.

- removes real provider credentials from the scenario/harness environment and transports the selected credential plus per-attempt attestation key over a bounded FD3 bootstrap
- exposes only a loopback model proxy and dummy provider credential to the real runtime
- enforces request, conservative input-byte, authoritative output-token, tool-call, response-size, model, route, redirect, and attempt budgets before or during dispatch
- uses endpoint-specific request allowlists to reject hosted tools/contexts, remote URLs, MCP, containers, shell/code execution, search/audio, premium routing, background modes, and multi-choice bypasses
- requires authoritative OpenAI/Anthropic usage receipts and HMAC-attests canonical meter receipts
- verifies exact chronological accepted-envelope prefixes and rejected suffixes, request bytes/hashes, token arithmetic, model identity, and output caps
- records typed failed receipts across the exact three-attempt policy
- adds black-box production FD3 parser tests for malformed/schema-invalid, oversized, held-open/truncated, and valid payloads

## Evidence

- Head: `7f2dd5be968`
- Base: `cbd4c2f8a82` (#28936)
- Six implementation commits replayed patch-identically onto the base; parser coverage is a separate seventh commit
- Independent P0/P1 review: CLEAN
- Live-model meter: 64/64
- Parent + preload network guards: 10/10
- Production FD3 parser: 5/5, 27 assertions
- Parser review rerun: 5/5 in 4.31s
- Biome, guide parity, and diff checks pass
- Deterministic keyless exact lane passed first attempt and 3/3 before the final parser-only extraction

Shared-host subprocess reruns had only timeout/OS `EPERM` teardown failures; no credential, FD, receipt, HMAC, or request-policy assertion regressed. A loaded-host provider timeout case passed alone in 613ms.

## Security boundary

This PR removes direct environment and descriptor inheritance, but it does **not** claim hostile-code certification. Same-UID Linux `/proc/<pid>/environ` inspection and raw socket/network bypass remain possible unless the scenario process runs in the OS sandbox tracked by #26821. Therefore the credentialed GitHub lane must remain disabled until #26821 lands and proves distinct process identity/proc isolation plus network enforcement.

No live-model request was made.

Fixes #25518. Refs #25268, #26821, and #22896.5f:T1316,# Relates to

Refs #21729 and #29488. Includes unmerged prerequisites from #30781 and #30792 because this draft targets develop.

# Change

Adds optional encrypted tenant PostgreSQL WAL/PITR configuration to the actual Ubuntu host bootstrap. A separate sensitive repository bundle is required; multiline configuration injection, Terraform state buckets and reuse of the logical-backup expiry bucket are rejected. New-host bootstrap configures synchronous encrypted WAL uploads with TLS verification, verifies the stanza and archive delivery, and establishes a full backup before scheduled backups/checks can run.

Weekly full and daily differential backups use systemd timers. The check must prove current WAL delivery and healthy current-generation backup receipts; it rejects completed backups older than 26 hours, full backups older than nine days, future timestamps, missing backups and repository errors. Explicit config includes prevent unrelated fragments from overriding managed configuration. No WAL queue-size discard cutoff is configured. Automatic expiry remains disabled pending independent deletion authority and restore proof.

# Verification

- Exact rebased head 54718f4e6cb0b2ff005ac5b04ded6d4e705ac0c6 passed root verification after install. Infrastructure source was unchanged by rebase.
- Infrastructure suite: 124 passed, 0 failed, 752 assertions. Native Terraform: 8 plans passed, including unsafe repository-input rejection. Actual rendered YAML parsed and extracted check script matched the tested source byte-for-byte.
- Rendered pgBackRest backup policy passed real PostgreSQL 16.15/pgBackRest 2.59.1 recovery over a local TLS S3 fixture with bad-credential and untrusted-CA rejection. Fixture-only identity, paths and credentials were substituted.
- Generated host files ran in a separate Ubuntu 24.04 VM with pgBackRest 2.50 and PostgreSQL16. An exclusively fixture-file-backed loop volume exercised exact-device bootstrap and one-use initialization. Restart preserved data. Actual systemd initialization, full backup, check and schedule readback passed.
- Separate Linux candidate restored a post-backup update and insert from WAL while the source remained deleted. A repository access failure produced check exit 41; restored access returned exit 0. All fixture timers/candidates and the VM were stopped afterward.

# Known gaps and deployment

Draft only. Linux proof used encrypted local storage; TLS S3 proof used a separate local fixture. Neither establishes real off-host storage durability, deployed credential scope, incident alert delivery, immutable retention or measured production RPO/RTO. Continuous control-plane and agent-state backup scope remains separate work. The check thresholds assess the daily/weekly schedule, not five-minute RPO.

Cloud-init is new-host bootstrap, not an in-place activation mechanism. Review any Terraform replacement and perform the documented data/role migration and write-fenced cutover. Null repository configuration leaves PITR inactive. Repository keys exist in sensitive Terraform state and cloud-init user data and need independently recoverable custody.

The first Linux drill placed its restore point before the fixture transaction committed and failed its assertion. The corrected drill committed before creating the restore point and passed into a new candidate; the failure was in the harness, not counted as successful recovery.

No deployment, live data migration, resource deletion or merge performed. No maintainer CI exception requested.
60:T1bad,Failed provider deletion must remain retryable after a node is disabled, and replacement creation must retain enough identity to clean up an uncertain outcome. This change persists retirement intent, verifies provider absence before ownership removal, and retains exact failed-create cleanup authority. Recovery also fails closed when prior state cannot be restored and publishes readiness only after restoration succeeds.

Refs #30675 and #30697. This PR covers lifecycle cleanup and state-preserving recovery. Funded automatic resume, backup activation, environment separation and live fleet cleanup remain separate work.

Persists cleanup/retirement authority and requires prior state restoration before recovery publishes a usable agent.

Bug fixes to existing cloud lifecycle behavior.

# Change

Development routing verification now recognizes api-development.eliza.app and rejects health responses identifying another tier, including in permissive rollout mode. The endpoint still requires real deployment and binding readback.

The nightly tenant backup reads back the uploaded encrypted archive and metadata and verifies their SHA-256 before listing or expiring older recovery points. A corrupted or unreadable upload fails without deleting older backups.

Adds reproducible PostgreSQL 16 recovery drills using pgBackRest: a full backup followed by WAL-only writes, a named recovery point, destructive fixture deletion, and restoration into a separate cluster. A second drill uses an authenticated TLS S3 fixture, rejects incorrect credentials and an untrusted certificate, and cleans up its owned local processes. The experimental rclone S3 server is a test fixture only.

62:Tf8f,The system APK could boot an agent its WebView could not call, route local inference into the wrong native loader, and terminate streamed replies when the request body ended. Preserve reflected Android service methods, route IPC through the initialized API kernel, and keep response lifetime independent of request-body completion. Headless Android plugin loading, canonical app-data paths, Direct Boot handling, and atomic installation identity support the privileged image runtime.

Android Kokoro also sent raw text into a build without eSpeak, producing garbled speech. Talk Mode now obtains IPA from the authenticated local agent using the bundled phonemizer, then sends it through UDS to JNI's existing IPA synthesis API. Phonemization errors reject playback rather than silently switching to system TTS. The path explicitly supports en-US and rejects more than 508 IPA code points, reserving two padding tokens within the pinned native graph's 510-token cap. JNI consumes exact-size allocated PCM and frees it through the native API.

Native packaging uses a shared C++ runtime and verifies hashes of actual packaged bytes. The native gitlink changes exactly one commit, [1891089db](https://github.com/elizaOS/llama.cpp/commit/1891089dbed568cec30b5b9591cba9b85553e3e9), after 6543d9078051a9bb194c2ef5c2995f003c5158de. Its Vulkan guard retains the storageBuffer16BitAccess requirement, requests VK_KHR_16bit_storage when advertised, and otherwise requires its promoted Vulkan 1.1 core capability. Human inspection remains required before merge. The TTS fix adds no native fork changes.

Bun staging now pins immutable GitHub asset IDs, upstream revisions, ZIP hashes, and executable hashes for stable and canary x64/arm64 artifacts. Downloads and local overrides verify both layers; every cached executable is rehashed. Packaged provenance reports the actual version and revision. The pinned canary binaries match the archived APK17 inputs exactly.

# Risks

Medium: updates the durable workflow runtime and native Codex adapter, and changes retained-session lifecycle decisions. Custom adapter commands remain honored. Smithers database migrations execute through its existing runtime. Approval, signal, cancellation, delayed event delivery, persistence, ownership and retained-session races are covered explicitly.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Updates both Smithers consumers to `smthrs@0.35.0` and the managed native Codex adapter to `@agentclientprotocol/codex-acp@1.10.0`. The old adapter could not run the configured Codex model. Native adapter diagnostics now use negotiated typed events rather than contaminating model answer text.

A completed Smithers task now retains a usable follow-up session without the watchdog prompting it while idle or admission reclaiming it during a prompt. Durable task consumers consult live ACP state. Idle reclamation claims the session atomically, and adapter-defined executing states remain monitored.

Workflow Runs and History distinguish loading, empty and failed states, support retries, retain visible polling errors, and reject superseded reads. Switching workflows resets editor state. A delayed list response cannot erase a newly started run. Projects retains mutation errors through polling, retries project loading and fits empty states into short viewports.

The UI inventory now ignores known generated caches and temporary inventory workspaces. This prevents local Vite output from changing the checked-in report or racing fixture teardown in CI; maintained hidden sources such as Storybook stay governed.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fixes, dependency updates and UI reliability improvements.

# Documentation changes needed?

Updated plugin workflow validation scenarios and the orchestrator adapter configuration documentation, including paired CLAUDE/AGENTS guides.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

Use the attached walkthrough and screenshots, then read the live trajectory: the model returns a unique nonce through Smithers, recalls it on a second prompt in the same native ACP session, an attempted reclaim during the follow-up is rejected, and that idle session is reclaimed successfully.

## Detailed testing steps

- Run `bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-workflow test`: 124 passing tests across all 16 isolated files. Real Smithers children persist and resume approval and signal waits; dependent tasks receive complete large Unicode output; a completed run does not replay model calls.
- Exercise slow event delivery, cancellation and callback deadlines. The #30519 tests reproduce four failures before the fix and pass afterward.
- Exercise retained-session follow-ups, retry/stop, watchdog and queue admission. The #28459 watchdog/admission tests reproduce five failures before the fix; all 293 tests in its six affected files pass with the qualified implementation.
- Inject History HTTP 503 in desktop and mobile browser fixtures, retry, and observe an explicit empty state. Exercise create/save/run/cancel/restore and Projects lifecycle controls.
- Run the real local workflow browser journey: create, persist, trigger, execute, render the widget, reload and recover the saved workflow.
- Owning-package suites: UI 13,631 passed / 7 skipped across 1,278 files; orchestrator 3,155 passed / 11 skipped across 285 files; Projects 254 passed across 33 files; workflows 124 passed across 16 files. That is 17,164 passing tests, excluding duplicate focused runs. The separate live proof covers the credentialed Codex path. Root verification and app visual/OCR results are attached below.

65:T1799,# Relates to

Part of #29488. This draft implements isolated Terraform tiers and safe tenant-database bootstrap; it does not close the full delivery-tier issue.

# Background

The apps Terraform roots previously shared database/network state across tiers, and the database bootstrap could select an unintended volume or initialize storage without explicit fresh-volume authorization. Each tier now selects its own state bucket, resource names, network, database host, and credentials. The consumer rejects remote state from a different environment. Development is admitted for the control plane and both apps roots.

Database startup requires the exact assigned ext4 volume and PostgreSQL 16. It rejects conflicting mounts, symlinks, mismatched database versions, and unexplained existing data. Initializing an empty volume additionally requires a short-lived, root-owned, one-use authorization receipt; ordinary restarts of existing databases require no receipt. The service retries transient readiness failures without formatting storage.

Change type: infrastructure bug fixes and development-tier support.

# Sync with develop

- [x] Rebased onto `b676280384b15df54204276e34bb8740db26936f`; zero conflicts.
- [x] Pinned Bun 1.3.14 / Node 24.15.0 installation and repository verification passed on the source tree represented by this head.
- [ ] Live infrastructure behavior verified. This remains a draft pending the evidence below.

# Risks

High operational impact when applied. New environment backends are candidate infrastructure, not a migration of the existing shared state. Do not apply the new roots against the legacy backend. Existing data and DNS require reviewed backup/restore, ownership/import, write fencing, and cutover. No apply, DNS change, database migration, or deletion was performed.

# Documentation changes needed?

Updated all three root READMEs with tier selection, state separation, migration order, and the fresh-volume authorization procedure. Nightly database dumps are explicitly insufficient for the proposed five-minute PITR objective.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

Review the apps-shared bootstrap and its behavioral harness, then the native Terraform remote-state environment tests and workflow credential-selection tests.

## Detailed testing steps

Use Terraform 1.10.5 and the repository-pinned Bun/Node versions. Run `terraform init -backend=false`, `terraform validate`, and `terraform test` in each of the three touched Terraform roots; tests use mocked providers and plan assertions. Run the infra package tests and the cloud deployment environment contract suite, followed by `bun run verify`.

- Native Terraform tests: control plane 4 passed; apps data plane 4 passed; apps shared 5 passed. Apps-shared validation and its 5 tests were repeated after the bootstrap edits.
- Infrastructure package: 109 passed, 0 failed, 720 assertions across 9 files, including 17 bootstrap behavior tests.
- Workflow environment contract: 23 passed, 380 assertions.

- Repository `bun run verify`: exit 0. Guide parity: 160 pairs; touched README local paths and `git diff --check` passed.

The shell harness exercises temporary files and actual hard-link exclusion, while block-device, PostgreSQL, and systemd boundaries use fixtures. These results are not a live boot, restore drill, or provider-isolation proof.

67:T3c3d,# Relates to

Relates to #30679. Buffered speech could split unfinished words, silently discard a rewritten final reply, and wait until one clip ended before synthesizing the next. Longer local Kokoro replies also silently stopped after the native 510-phoneme limit.

This change retains incomplete lexical tails, rejects incompatible final rewrites visibly, prepares the next clip while preserving one ordered playback owner, and propagates speech failure/recovery through the app shell. Kokoro now preflights the complete utterance, splits oversized phrases losslessly at word boundaries, and synthesizes the exact checked IPA through ABI v14+. Indivisible oversized input, empty phonemes, unsupported old ABI and exhausted output capacity fail explicitly. Cancellation prevents stale playback and later segment dispatch.

# Sync with develop

Head `9743eb1a4cb94a0f3d14020675e4365f5121c2b0`, cleanly rebased onto `cd821540d73c4e7370a9407c07be19020a3b810b`. Post-sync install passed with the existing fused inference installation retained. Post-sync `bun run verify` passed: all 376 Turbo tasks and all repository audits, exit 0.

# Risks

The buffered web queue serves local inference, Eliza Cloud and ElevenLabs. Live evidence below covers the configured local Kokoro path; other providers and physical native devices remain unverified. Encoded lookahead avoids another synthesis request but is not a peak-memory guarantee. Canonical speakable text is preserved; the existing sanitizer still removes unsupported markup/URLs/emoji. Legacy native libraries without the checked IPA entry now return an actionable error instead of using an unverified truncating path.

# Documentation

Updated local-inference compatibility guidance and contracts for checked IPA synthesis. Shared phrase-cache eligibility/identity remains owned by #30680.

68:T19a4,# Relates to

Prerequisite for #23094; does not close it.

# Background

Entitlement publication now verifies the organization's canonical subscription identity and current immutable lifecycle revision before replay or projection CAS. A historical subscription cannot overwrite or replay the current account's projection. Grace uses its actual deadline; canceled/free projections retain the transition that produced them.

A canonical account association is owned by the existing lifecycle repository. New identities require the command-captured expected identity. Migration chooses the unique live subscription or sole historical identity; ambiguous terminal history becomes unavailable rather than being ordered by timestamps. Irreversible account erasure releases the association in the same transaction before deleting history.

# Sync with develop

Head `09d3fa484a47f4fc3beaa6f19b7dc1498c9afe44`, base `cd821540d73c4e7370a9407c07be19020a3b810b`. The prerequisite patch remains unchanged after rebase. Pinned Bun 1.3.14 install passed. Exact-head root `bun run verify` passed 376/376 tasks and all subsequent repository audits, exit 0.

Live merge-target readback at evidence publication: `65a4f5216421ba74a75b1bc9bc98de0ae2d1ceb1`. That later develop tip is not mislabeled as the base used for the local root run; GitHub reports the exact PR head conflict-free. No bypass is requested.

# Risks

This adds a billing-authority migration. Ambiguous existing terminal history requires reconciliation; no guessed identity is chosen. Paid resource ceilings remain nullable, not unlimited. Full enforcement, generic trial/payer/access policy and real Dedicated continuity are outside this prerequisite.

No production migration, deployed provider or money operation is claimed. Local PGlite tests exercise real database constraints and transaction rollback; they do not substitute for independent PostgreSQL sessions. All five exact-head hosted checks passed in [run 34020529015](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/34020529015), including the real PostgreSQL authority job, billing replay E2E, Windows security, source smoke and aggregate gate. A readiness-triggered run, if created, must also finish before merge.

No rendered UI or public endpoint changes. Remaining full-issue acceptance is tracked in #23094.

# Risks

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Completed keyed effects now retire only their own prior confirmation preview. Explicitly pending retryable work receives another planner decision when evaluator protocol parsing fails, instead of silently ending. Replies that assert an empty tracked state require matching read proof, including possessive wording and quoted result assertions; actual examples and uncertainty remain distinct. Explicit user conditions such as “If you want, say go and I'll retry” preserve the complete honest reply.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fixes to planner continuation and reply validation; no new public API or database migration.

# Documentation changes needed?

No public configuration or API change; regression tests and evidence describe the existing contracts restored.

'' : `/${page + 1}`}`;</code>.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><a href="https://redirect.github.com/biomejs/biome/pull/11440">#11440</a> <a href="https://github.com/biomejs/biome/commit/b88f1ea368c727ff8ebeddcaf9448f6ddc7c4878"><code>b88f1ea</code></a> Thanks <a href="https://github.com/Princesseuh"><code>@​Princesseuh</code></a>! - Fixed a quote inside a regex character class breaking the rest of an Astro file, such as <code>const unsafe = /[/&quot;]/;</code>.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><a href="https://redirect.github.com/biomejs/biome/pull/11508">#11508</a> <a href="https://github.com/biomejs/biome/commit/54f3a2ea2765c80df11d04e47cd8da0354f5f7ca"><code>54f3a2e</code></a> Thanks <a href="https://github.com/dyc3"><code>@​dyc3</code></a>! - Added the nursery rule <a href="https://biomejs.dev/linter/rules/use-flat-math-min-max/"><code>useFlatMathMinMax</code></a>. Because <code>Math.min()</code> and <code>Math.max()</code> accept any number of arguments, the rule reports unnecessary nested calls to the same method:</p>
<pre lang="js"><code>Math.max(Math.max(a, b), c);
</code></pre>
<p>The fix flattens this expression to <code>Math.max(a, b, c)</code>.</p>
</li>

<h2>2.5.12</h2>
<h3>Patch Changes</h3>
<ul>
<li>
<p><a href="https://redirect.github.com/biomejs/biome/pull/11440">#11440</a> <a href="https://github.com/biomejs/biome/commit/b88f1ea368c727ff8ebeddcaf9448f6ddc7c4878"><code>b88f1ea</code></a> Thanks <a href="https://github.com/Princesseuh"><code>@​Princesseuh</code></a>! - Fixed Astro attribute expressions rejecting TypeScript and JSX syntax that is accepted in text expressions.</p>
<pre lang="astro"><code>&lt;Component icon={&lt;Icon /&gt;} count={total as number} onSelect={(e: Event) =&gt; e} /&gt;
</code></pre>
</li>
<li>
<p><a href="https://redirect.github.com/biomejs/biome/pull/11440">#11440</a> <a href="https://github.com/biomejs/biome/commit/b88f1ea368c727ff8ebeddcaf9448f6ddc7c4878"><code>b88f1ea</code></a> Thanks <a href="https://github.com/Princesseuh"><code>@​Princesseuh</code></a>! - Fixed Astro attribute names being split on <code>:</code> and <code>.</code> inside an expression, such as <code>{x &amp;&amp; &lt;button x-on:keyup.enter={go} client:load.foo /&gt;}</code>.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><a href="https://redirect.github.com/biomejs/biome/pull/11440">#11440</a> <a href="https://github.com/biomejs/biome/commit/b88f1ea368c727ff8ebeddcaf9448f6ddc7c4878"><code>b88f1ea</code></a> Thanks <a href="https://github.com/Princesseuh"><code>@​Princesseuh</code></a>! - Fixed a bare <code>&gt;</code> in the children of an Astro expression being treated as markup, such as <code>{x &amp;&amp; &lt;div&gt;a &gt; b&lt;/div&gt;}</code>.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><a href="https://redirect.github.com/biomejs/biome/pull/11440">#11440</a> <a href="https://github.com/biomejs/biome/commit/b88f1ea368c727ff8ebeddcaf9448f6ddc7c4878"><code>b88f1ea</code></a> Thanks <a href="https://github.com/Princesseuh"><code>@​Princesseuh</code></a>! - Fixed HTML comments inside an Astro expression failing to parse. They are now read as trivia, wherever they appear among the children.</p>
<pre lang="astro"><code>{x &amp;&amp; &lt;div&gt;&lt;!-- first --&gt;text&lt;!-- last --&gt;&lt;/div&gt;}
{cond &amp;&amp; &lt;a&gt;&lt;/a&gt;&lt;!-- c --&gt;&lt;b&gt;&lt;/b&gt;}
</code></pre>
</li>
<li>
<p><a href="https://redirect.github.com/biomejs/biome/pull/11440">#11440</a> <a href="https://github.com/biomejs/biome/commit/b88f1ea368c727ff8ebeddcaf9448f6ddc7c4878"><code>b88f1ea</code></a> Thanks <a href="https://github.com/Princesseuh"><code>@​Princesseuh</code></a>! - Fixed <code>is:raw</code> children inside an Astro expression being read as JSX, such as <code>{x &amp;&amp; &lt;div is:raw&gt;{not js} &lt; &amp; text&lt;/div&gt;}</code>.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><a href="https://redirect.github.com/biomejs/biome/pull/11440">#11440</a> <a href="https://github.com/biomejs/biome/commit/b88f1ea368c727ff8ebeddcaf9448f6ddc7c4878"><code>b88f1ea</code></a> Thanks <a href="https://github.com/Princesseuh"><code>@​Princesseuh</code></a>! - Fixed an apostrophe or quote in the text of a JSX element inside an Astro expression ending the expression early, such as <code>{items.map((i) =&gt; &lt;li&gt;it's {i}&lt;/li&gt;)}</code>.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><a href="https://redirect.github.com/biomejs/biome/pull/11440">#11440</a> <a href="https://github.com/biomejs/biome/commit/b88f1ea368c727ff8ebeddcaf9448f6ddc7c4878"><code>b88f1ea</code></a> Thanks <a href="https://github.com/Princesseuh"><code>@​Princesseuh</code></a>! - Fixed the children of a <code>&lt;script&gt;</code> or <code>&lt;style&gt;</code> inside an Astro expression being read as JSX. Their contents are text, so braces and comparisons no longer have to be escaped.</p>
<pre lang="astro"><code>{cond &amp;&amp; &lt;style&gt;a { color: red }&lt;/style&gt;}
{cond &amp;&amp; &lt;script&gt;let x = {a: 1};&lt;/script&gt;}
</code></pre>
</li>

</li>

<p><a href="https://redirect.github.com/biomejs/biome/pull/11440">#11440</a> <a href="https://github.com/biomejs/biome/commit/b88f1ea368c727ff8ebeddcaf9448f6ddc7c4878"><code>b88f1ea</code></a> Thanks <a href="https://github.com/Princesseuh"><code>@​Princesseuh</code></a>! - Fixed unquoted attribute values being rejected inside an Astro expression, such as <code>{x &amp;&amp; &lt;a class=foo maxlength=255 href=/about&gt;go&lt;/a&gt;}</code>.</p>

Updates `@vitejs/plugin-react` from 6.0.5 to 6.1.1
<details>
<summary>Release notes</summary>
<p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/vitejs/vite-plugin-react/releases">@​vitejs/plugin-react's releases</a>.</em></p>
<blockquote>
<h2>plugin-react@6.1.1</h2>
<h3>Add <code>compiler.logDiagnostics</code> option</h3>
<p>Recoverable React Compiler diagnostics are no longer logged by default. Set <code>compiler.logDiagnostics</code> to <code>true</code> to log them through Vite. Fatal diagnostics are always logged and fail the transform.</p>
<h3>Respect environment sourcemap option for React Compiler transform when <code>builder.sharedPlugins</code> is enabled (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/vitejs/vite-plugin-react/pull/1439">#1439</a>)</h3>
<p>The React Compiler transform was using the top-level sourcemap option instead of the environment sourcemap option. This caused a problem when the experimental <code>builder.sharedPlugins</code> was enabled.</p>
<h2>plugin-react@6.1.0</h2>
<h3>Add experimental native React Compiler support (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/vitejs/vite-plugin-react/pull/1419">#1419</a>)</h3>
<p>Add experimental native React Compiler support.</p>
<p>You can use it by installing <code>oxc-transform-react</code> and enabling it via the <code>compiler</code> option:</p>
<pre lang="sh"><code>npm install -D oxc-transform-react
</code></pre>
<pre lang="js"><code>import { defineConfig } from 'vite'
import react from '@vitejs/plugin-react'
<p>export default defineConfig({<br />
plugins: [<br />
react({ compiler: true })<br />
]<br />
})<br />
</code></pre></p>
</blockquote>
</details>
<details>

Updates `@vitest/coverage-v8` from 4.1.10 to 5.0.0
<details>
<summary>Release notes</summary>
<p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/vitest-dev/vitest/releases">@​vitest/coverage-v8's releases</a>.</em></p>
<blockquote>
<h2>v5.0.0</h2>
<p>Vitest 5 is officially out! This release focuses on performance and brings a lot of new features while fixing long-standing bugs. See our <a href="https://vitest.dev/blog/vitest-5.html">blog post</a> for the official announcement.</p>
<h3>   🚨 Breaking Changes</h3>

Updates `bun-types` from 1.3.14 to 1.4.0
<details>
<summary>Release notes</summary>
<p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/oven-sh/bun/releases">bun-types's releases</a>.</em></p>
<blockquote>
<h2>Bun v1.4</h2>
<p>To install Bun v1.4</p>
<pre lang="bash"><code>curl -fsSL https://bun.com/install | bash
# or you can use npm
# npm install -g bun
</code></pre>
<p>Windows:</p>
<pre lang="bash"><code>powershell -c &quot;irm bun.com/install.ps1|iex&quot;
</code></pre>
<p>To upgrade to Bun v1.4.0:</p>
<pre lang="bash"><code>bun upgrade
</code></pre>
<p>Read the <a href="https://bun.com/1.4">blog post</a>:
<!-- raw HTML omitted -->
<!-- raw HTML omitted -->
<!-- raw HTML omitted --></p>

Updates `@biomejs/biome` from 2.5.8 to 2.5.11
<details>
<summary>Release notes</summary>
<p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/biomejs/biome/releases">@​biomejs/biome's releases</a>.</em></p>
<blockquote>
<h2>Biome CLI v2.5.11</h2>
<h2>2.5.11</h2>
<h3>Patch Changes</h3>
<ul>
<li>
<p><a href="https://redirect.github.com/biomejs/biome/pull/11499">#11499</a> <a href="https://github.com/biomejs/biome/commit/9743d0ceae807757300e5b0ec66266125bc664c1"><code>9743d0c</code></a> Thanks <a href="https://github.com/scs0209"><code>@​scs0209</code></a>! - Fixed <a href="https://redirect.github.com/biomejs/biome/issues/11496">#11496</a>: <a href="https://biomejs.dev/linter/rules/use-valid-anchor/"><code>useValidAnchor</code></a> now treats Astro JSX shorthand attributes like <code>&lt;a {href}&gt;</code> as a valid <code>href</code>.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><a href="https://redirect.github.com/biomejs/biome/pull/11437">#11437</a> <a href="https://github.com/biomejs/biome/commit/88f805e19b67ab4c876e4fc4a8b4018bd03df20b"><code>88f805e</code></a> Thanks <a href="https://github.com/Princesseuh"><code>@​Princesseuh</code></a>! - Fixed <a href="https://redirect.github.com/biomejs/biome/issues/9944">#9944</a>: adjacent elements inside an Astro expression now parse as an implicit fragment instead of raising an error.</p>
<pre lang="astro"><code>{options.map(() =&gt;
  &lt;div /&gt;
  &lt;div /&gt;
)}
</code></pre>
</li>
<li>
<p><a href="https://redirect.github.com/biomejs/biome/pull/11437">#11437</a> <a href="https://github.com/biomejs/biome/commit/88f805e19b67ab4c876e4fc4a8b4018bd03df20b"><code>88f805e</code></a> Thanks <a href="https://github.com/Princesseuh"><code>@​Princesseuh</code></a>! - Fixed Astro templates rejecting unclosed HTML void elements, such as <code>{cond &amp;&amp; &lt;br&gt;}</code>.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><a href="https://redirect.github.com/biomejs/biome/pull/11507">#11507</a> <a href="https://github.com/biomejs/biome/commit/e2fc03669886732ac9c768aa00f3ffae46241848"><code>e2fc036</code></a> Thanks <a href="https://github.com/dyc3"><code>@​dyc3</code></a>! - Fixed <a href="https://redirect.github.com/biomejs/biome/issues/11157">#11157</a>: <a href="https://biomejs.dev/linter/rules/no-unused-variables/"><code>noUnusedVariables</code></a> no longer reports Vue <code>&lt;script setup&gt;</code> bindings used by CSS <code>v-bind()</code> as unused.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><a href="https://redirect.github.com/biomejs/biome/pull/11398">#11398</a> <a href="https://github.com/biomejs/biome/commit/afc4615079a72e0810f06186f78e5cb177a5638e"><code>afc4615</code></a> Thanks <a href="https://github.com/dyc3"><code>@​dyc3</code></a>! - Fixed <a href="https://redirect.github.com/biomejs/biome/issues/11389">#11389</a>: Files passed through <code>--stdin-file-path</code> now use full HTML support for Astro, Svelte, and Vue when it is enabled.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><a href="https://redirect.github.com/biomejs/biome/pull/11526">#11526</a> <a href="https://github.com/biomejs/biome/commit/372cd68ba156efdc497017598a49e21484a3ada9"><code>372cd68</code></a> Thanks <a href="https://github.com/dyc3"><code>@​dyc3</code></a>! - Fixed <a href="https://biomejs.dev/linter/rules/no-vue-ref-as-operand/"><code>noVueRefAsOperand</code></a> to track Vue refs through declaration aliases and <code>toRefs()</code> properties, and to recognize <code>useTemplateRef()</code> results. The rule no longer reports false positives such as plain ref transfers, plain <code>toRefs()</code> property access, <code>defineModel()</code> modifiers, or the supported <code>.effect</code> member as operands.</p>
<p>The refactor enabling these fixes also improves the performance of the rule.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><a href="https://redirect.github.com/biomejs/biome/pull/11458">#11458</a> <a href="https://github.com/biomejs/biome/commit/a7cd2868c3d98fbc75174c5bf69fdcfacbe87304"><code>a7cd286</code></a> Thanks <a href="https://github.com/dyc3"><code>@​dyc3</code></a>! - Fixed <a href="https://redirect.github.com/biomejs/biome/issues/11436">#11436</a>: GritQL snippets such as <code>export { $specifiers } from $source</code> now match named re-exports with aliases, inline <code>type</code> modifiers, and multiple specifiers.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><a href="https://redirect.github.com/biomejs/biome/pull/11515">#11515</a> <a href="https://github.com/biomejs/biome/commit/382b15d5e0f9e2a75604fec1f15ad342628c99c7"><code>382b15d</code></a> Thanks <a href="https://github.com/dyc3"><code>@​dyc3</code></a>! - Fixed <a href="https://redirect.github.com/biomejs/biome/issues/11390">#11390</a>, where <code>noFloatingPromises</code> performed expensive full type inference for calls to non-Promise methods declared on third-party TypeScript classes. The rule now classifies those calls using targeted type information.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><a href="https://redirect.github.com/biomejs/biome/pull/11516">#11516</a> <a href="https://github.com/biomejs/biome/commit/6f40e8224afc58737626d997c0ea779e2b37baca"><code>6f40e82</code></a> Thanks <a href="https://github.com/levrik"><code>@​levrik</code></a>! - Fixed <a href="https://biomejs.dev/linter/rules/no-vue-ref-as-operand/"><code>noVueRefAsOperand</code></a> so it no longer reports a callback parameter (e.g. from <code>.find()</code>, <code>.map()</code>) as an unwrapped ref value just because it's nested inside a <code>ref()</code>, <code>computed()</code>, or similar call.</p>
<pre lang="js"><code>const result = computed(() =&gt; list.find((item) =&gt; item.label === &quot;a&quot;));
</code></pre>
<p>Previously, <code>item</code> here was incorrectly treated as a ref value because the rule attributed it to the outer <code>computed()</code> call.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><a href="https://redirect.github.com/biomejs/biome/pull/11495">#11495</a> <a href="https://github.com/biomejs/biome/commit/496268d1470b6a4cd52bff03d37899c4bb7f77ab"><code>496268d</code></a> Thanks <a href="https://github.com/Netail"><code>@​Netail</code></a>! - Fixed <a href="https://biomejs.dev/linter/rules/use-graphql-naming-convention/"><code>useGraphqlNamingConvention</code></a> so it no longer reports GraphQL enum value definitions with comments &amp; descriptions and now displays a more accurate diagnostic range.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><a href="https://redirect.github.com/biomejs/biome/pull/11407">#11407</a> <a href="https://github.com/biomejs/biome/commit/6ef52b02fc07e23de7ad498d853aaf2c825717fd"><code>6ef52b0</code></a> Thanks <a href="https://github.com/1678092075"><code>@​1678092075</code></a>! - Fixed <a href="https://redirect.github.com/biomejs/biome/issues/11214">#11214</a>: <a href="https://biomejs.dev/linter/rules/no-unused-variables/"><code>noUnusedVariables</code></a> no longer reports type parameters declared by non-default function overload signatures that have an implementation.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><a href="https://redirect.github.com/biomejs/biome/pull/11322">#11322</a> <a href="https://github.com/biomejs/biome/commit/5c353e6c85a40807e2710684da807f8757ee650c"><code>5c353e6</code></a> Thanks <a href="https://github.com/jp-knj"><code>@​jp-knj</code></a>! - Added a new nursery rule <code>noAstroSetHtmlDirective</code>, which disallows Astro's <code>set:html</code> directive because untrusted content can introduce cross-site scripting vulnerabilities.</p>
<p>For example, the following snippet triggers the rule:</p>
<pre lang="astro"><code>&lt;div set:html={content} /&gt;
</code></pre>
</li>
</ul>

</blockquote>

Updates `@testing-library/react` from 16.3.2 to 16.3.3
<details>
<summary>Release notes</summary>
<p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/testing-library/react-testing-library/releases">@​testing-library/react's releases</a>.</em></p>
<blockquote>
<h2>v16.3.3</h2>
<h2><a href="https://github.com/testing-library/react-testing-library/compare/v16.3.2...v16.3.3">16.3.3</a> (2026-08-27)</h2>
<h3>Bug Fixes</h3>
<ul>
<li>Avoid act() re-entrant when dispatching events (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/testing-library/react-testing-library/issues/1468">#1468</a>) (<a href="https://github.com/testing-library/react-testing-library/commit/20ce75f2907ca0e5c5a8ae595c0e9a4e368c7800">20ce75f</a>)</li>
</ul>
</blockquote>
</details>
<details>
<summary>Commits</summary>
<ul>

Updates `@vitejs/plugin-react` from 6.0.5 to 6.1.1
<details>
<summary>Release notes</summary>
<p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/vitejs/vite-plugin-react/releases">@​vitejs/plugin-react's releases</a>.</em></p>
<blockquote>
<h2>plugin-react@6.1.1</h2>
<h3>Add <code>compiler.logDiagnostics</code> option</h3>
<p>Recoverable React Compiler diagnostics are no longer logged by default. Set <code>compiler.logDiagnostics</code> to <code>true</code> to log them through Vite. Fatal diagnostics are always logged and fail the transform.</p>
<h3>Respect environment sourcemap option for React Compiler transform when <code>builder.sharedPlugins</code> is enabled (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/vitejs/vite-plugin-react/pull/1439">#1439</a>)</h3>
<p>The React Compiler transform was using the top-level sourcemap option instead of the environment sourcemap option. This caused a problem when the experimental <code>builder.sharedPlugins</code> was enabled.</p>
<h2>plugin-react@6.1.0</h2>
<h3>Add experimental native React Compiler support (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/vitejs/vite-plugin-react/pull/1419">#1419</a>)</h3>
<p>Add experimental native React Compiler support.</p>
<p>You can use it by installing <code>oxc-transform-react</code> and enabling it via the <code>compiler</code> option:</p>
<pre lang="sh"><code>npm install -D oxc-transform-react
</code></pre>
<pre lang="js"><code>import { defineConfig } from 'vite'
import react from '@vitejs/plugin-react'
<p>export default defineConfig({<br />
plugins: [<br />
react({ compiler: true })<br />
]<br />
})<br />
</code></pre></p>
</blockquote>
</details>
<details>

## Summary

- makes the app/UI Documents surface at `/character/documents` and Relationships surface at `/apps/relationships` the canonical first-party renderers
- preserves `/relationships` as a tested compatibility alias and preserves the prior Relationships developer-only visibility contract
- removes the unregistered Documents UI exports and duplicate Relationships plugin declaration, bundle target, renderer, fixtures, and UI-only dependencies
- derives the ownership inventory from `BUILTIN_VIEWS`, reachable typed `Plugin.views`, `TAB_PATHS`, `LEGACY_PREFIX_TAB_ALIASES`, and production `registerAppShellPage` paths/pathPatterns
- fails on normalized id/path collisions and overlapping app-shell patterns; reported platforms now come from the canonical runtime declaration rather than a fabricated all-target list
- points the capability-density audit at the shipped `@elizaos/ui` Documents/Relationships renderers instead of deleted plugin trees
- gates compatibility aliases through the canonical destination, so `/relationships` cannot bypass `VITE_ENABLE_APPS=false`

## Ownership result

| Surface | Canonical owner | Canonical path | Compatibility |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Documents | app/UI Knowledge renderer + builtin declaration | `/character/documents` | plugin remains API/presenter/service only |
| Relationships | app/UI Relationships workspace + developer-gated builtin declaration | `/apps/relationships` | `/relationships` translates to the canonical tab; plugin remains action/provider/schema only |

6d:T12a8,# Relates to

Prerequisite for #23093; does not close it. Generic subscriber authority remains tracked in #30638.

# Background

Recurring Stripe deliveries previously fell through the one-time queue consumer. Subscription Checkout, subscription/invoice lifecycle events, and invoice-linked recurring payment/refund/dispute events now remain in the existing retry/DLQ path until a recurring finalizer can durably reconcile them. Invoice authority is retrieved before a linked payment can enter one-time fulfillment; provider lookup failures remain retryable regardless of their error text.

Basil payment and charge objects can omit invoice linkage. Missing or undefined linkage is conservatively retained, including expanded dispute charges. Explicit Acacia `invoice: null` preserves one-time purchase, auto-top-up, refund and dispute behavior. Legacy fixtures now model that authority explicitly.

# Sync with develop

Head `173a996249e2e226639acab773c2d2d7663d78c2` is rebased onto `cd821540d73c4e7370a9407c07be19020a3b810b`. All three issue patches are unchanged by rebase. Pinned Bun 1.3.14 install passed. Exact-head root `bun run verify` passed all 376 tasks and subsequent repository audits, exit 0.

# Risks

Provider outages delay invoice-linked deliveries. Ambiguous modern objects may represent a one-time purchase but remain retryable until authoritative classification is available. Retained deliveries reach the existing DLQ after five attempts. The Redis queue pops before requeue/DLQ writes; these tests do not establish crash-safe delivery during a storage failure.

This PR does not implement checkout/portal routes, recurring finalization, trial eligibility, allowance grants or refund policy. Real signed sandbox webhooks and deployed queue/ledger evidence remain part of #23093 acceptance. No production or Stripe sandbox mutation is claimed.

# Documentation changes needed?

No public API or rendered UI changes. Scope and remaining integration gates are recorded in this PR and #23093.

# Testing

- Exact current head: pinned install and root verify passed, 376/376 tasks plus repository audits.
- Independent exact-head review: clean; 82 tests passed, 302 assertions across seven consumer/compatibility files. All three patches match the prior reviewed revision.
- Source-equivalent fixture checkpoint `986e3b98bbb9c5bfbdcd89cccce49c3aae687ad8`: full isolated cloud API suite, all 595 files passed, exit 0; API types, router contract, bundle dry run and lint passed. These historical runs are not presented as newly executed current-head sweeps.

6e:Tb82,Relates to #30552.

The staging diagnostic previously counted worker-wide timeouts. This change correlates historical observations with the account-scoped Personal target using a SHA-256 digest of its canonical container name, without exporting that identity or raw logs.

Timeout attribution accepts only the standalone Docker or Tailnet worker message, binding its category and container in the same anchored match. Embedded log text cannot impersonate another target's timeout. Complete adjacent Bun diagnostic frames produce closed state observations; malformed or interleaved frames remain explicitly unparsed. The existing source/PID guards, read-only execution, deployment lock and staging admission remain intact.

- [x] Post-sync verification passed at `a5bd42f6f6e5a767a469f80752d98b6212ebe4c4`.

# Risks

Low. Template rendering now preserves executable bits without copying special permission bits. Upgrade repairs missing executable bits on otherwise unchanged managed files. Migration now rejects unreadable existing source paths; genuinely absent optional paths remain supported.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Generated projects were losing the executable bit on their codesign wrapper, so PATH selected the system binary and bypassed the wrapper. Rendering now preserves executable permissions, and upgrade repairs affected existing projects even when file contents already match. Packaging and rendering share the same ignored-directory inventory.

Migration previously treated filesystem failures as absent files and could export an incomplete agent without a warning. Read and discovery failures now carry an actionable `MIGRATION_SOURCE_READ_FAILED` error with path, operation and cause. Only `ENOENT` remains optional; existing SQLite availability warnings retain their contract.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fixes and internal consolidation.

# Documentation changes needed?

No user-facing command or archive format change. Source comments document permission and optional-file contracts.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

Inspect the generated-artifact receipt and complete installed-CLI runtime log attached below. They show wrapper dispatch before and after deliberate permission loss and repair, plus failed migration with neither requested output created.

## Detailed testing steps

At `a5bd42f6f6e5a767a469f80752d98b6212ebe4c4`: all 259 CLI tests passed; package typecheck, lint, build, packaged smoke and root `bun run verify` passed. Packaged smoke installs the actual CLI and exercises create and upgrade. Real filesystem tests cover unreadable files/directories, wrong-type paths, absent optional files, byte/hash preservation and dry-run behavior.

# Risks

Low to medium. Changes affect evaluator eligibility, cancellation guidance and the component publication envelope. No schema migration, transaction policy, hook retry or terminal-submission semantics are changed.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

A stashed-only form was admitted to an evaluator that requires an active session. Admission now matches the active/ready session contract, while the restore action retains ownership of stashed forms. A high-effort ready form now exposes cancellation confirmation through FORM_CONTEXT instead of prompting submission after a cancellation request.

The first real form write also failed because Drizzle could not inspect the serializer's null-prototype top-level value. Session, submission and autofill publication now use an ordinary top-level envelope after safe serialization and validation. Nested data, own `__proto__` keys and pre-write accessor rejection remain intact; the exported serializer contract is unchanged.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fixes and consolidation of existing ownership.

# Documentation changes needed?

No new endpoint, schema migration or product feature. Source comments describe the preserved compatibility boundaries.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

Review the actual boundary/output artifacts below, then the independent exact-file review receipt.

# Sync with develop

Base 9b314da5e65327702d3d02ad36684e7632ebe288. Independently owned CLI and form fixes landed on develop during verification; they do not touch the diagnostic files. Candidate f6ac33d8db1c8ab020911d48e75dee39e610090c.

# Risks

Medium: the diagnostic uses existing protected staging SSH authority to read the worker process environment and journal. It emits only a closed output schema: validated public image digest, source commit, service state, and fixed category counts. Raw logs, credentials, hostnames and agent identifiers remain inside the process. Invalid or changing source/process identity fails the read. The journal is worker-wide and does not identify an individual agent's cause.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Adds a `diagnose` dispatch choice to the existing provisioning-worker workflow. It requires `develop`, staging, and the exact currently installed worker SHA; rejects deployment/reconciler inputs; and skips the deploy job. The SSH step takes a shared lease on the existing deployment lock, reads the active worker's image pin and at most 10,000 journal records from 24 hours, and checks source/PID again before publishing. It neither writes host files nor restarts services nor changes database state. Normal deploy remains the default, preserving its run-name format and existing guards.

## What kind of change is this?

Operator diagnostics needed to investigate the unresolved staging provisioning failure.

# Documentation changes needed?

Updated the workflow README with invocation constraints, output scope and limits.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

Review admission before credential use, the fixed remote commands and lock, then the privacy boundary tests. The protected run is a post-merge observation, not a prerequisite that could be obtained by executing an unreviewed branch with credentials.

## Detailed testing steps

- Six Node tests pass: sensitive journal/environment data cannot escape, invalid source rejects before host access, source/PID replacement prevents publication, malformed journal input fails, and the real stdin CLI suppresses hostile input on failure.
- 78 existing provisioning workflow, environment/concurrency and effect-ledger contracts pass.
- Biome, actionlint and diff checks pass.
- Post-sync install passed in 113.76 seconds on the shared host. Final root `bun run verify` passed with exit 0, including the 11,447-file focused/skip audit. Six diagnostic tests and 78 deployment contracts passed again after rebase.
